## Reviewer Concerns

Disposition: COMPLETED

Stop: PASS

No blocking findings remain. The 37 open minor finding(s) below are recorded for the user, not fixed.

### R3-1 — clarity (minor)

**Problem**

> The Active rule says every figure in Success Criteria applies to active assignments only. That fits the 80% freshness check and the stop rule, which are Friday snapshots. It does not fit 'At least 70% of asks are answered in the app within one working day', which counts asks over four weeks. The rule does not say whether 'active' is judged when the ask was made or when the figure is computed. An ask answered with 'done' leaves its assignment inactive, and an ask cancelled by an owner change, or still pending when the firedrill is closed, sits on an inactive assignment. Example: 10 asks, of which 6 are answered within a day with 'done', 2 are answered within a day with 'on track' and 2 are never answered. Counted on the state when asked: 8 / 10 = 80%, target met. Counted on assignments still active when measured: 2 / 4 = 50%, target missed. `update_request` has no field that marks a cancelled ask. The board-opens, own-report and extraction criteria are not assignment figures at all. No stated decision hangs on the 70% figure, so this is recorded as minor.

**Remedy**

> Limit the sentence to the freshness figure and the stop rule. For the 70% figure, state that it counts every ask made in the period, on an assignment that was active when asked, and say whether asks cancelled by an owner change or by closing the firedrill are left out.

### R3-2 — consistency (minor)

**Problem**

> Two older rules still speak of all of a firedrill's assignments. Status: the board shows 'the worst status among its assignments'. Open and done: 'When every assignment is done' the firedrill shows as 'Done, ready to close'. Read literally, an owner who was blocked or at risk when replaced keeps the firedrill at that status and stops it from ever showing as ready to close, although the ended assignment 'drops off the board'. The Active rule covers stale, fresh, asks and the success figures, not these two rules.

**Remedy**

> Say that the status roll-up and the 'Done, ready to close' rule count only assignments that have not ended.

### R3-3 — consistency (minor)

**Problem**

> The Roles list still gives Lead 'reassign' only. Adding an owner and removing an owner, which the new rule grants to Lead, are not in it.

**Remedy**

> Change the Lead entry to 'add, remove or reassign owners'.

### R3-4 — completeness (minor)

**Problem**

> Nothing says what the former owner can do once their assignment has ended. The ask email they already hold links to the update screen, and a Manager may 'post updates on their own assignments'. If the screen still accepts an update, it lands on an ended assignment that is off the board, and the manager believes they have answered.

**Remedy**

> State that an ended assignment accepts no updates and that its update link tells the former owner the firedrill now belongs to someone else.

### R3-5 — completeness (minor)

**Problem**

> Reassigning also creates a new owner, who then sees the emails already shared with the firedrill's owners. The notice is promised for the add-owner step only, so a reassign can widen who sees an email with no reminder to the person doing it.

**Remedy**

> Say that the reassign step shows the same notice as the add-owner step.

### R3-6 — clarity (minor)

**Problem**

> The switch is 'set when it is confirmed or attached'. Whether it can be changed afterwards, and by whom, is not said. If it cannot, an email shared by mistake stays visible to the owners until the firedrill is closed.

**Remedy**

> State that Lead or Triage can turn sharing on or off for an email at any time while the firedrill is open.

### R3-7 — feasibility (minor)

**Problem**

> The 120-day window stops most wrong roll-overs into the next year, but not all. A date with no year that lies P days before the anchor resolves to 365 - P days after it, so it is filled whenever 365 - P is 120 or less, that is when the date passed 245 or more days before the message was sent. Example: a message sent 2026-11-03 says 'the January 15 deadline was missed'. 2026-01-15 is 292 days before the anchor (day 307 of the year minus day 15). The first January 15 on or after the anchor is 2027-01-15, which is 365 - 292 = 73 days later and inside the window, so the field is filled with 2027-01-15. The intended date is 2026-01-15. The case is rare and the confirm step shows the quote, but the stated bar is zero wrong due dates ('Empty is fine, wrong is not').

**Remedy**

> Fill a date with no year only when it resolves inside the anchor's own calendar year and list any date that needs a roll-over into the next year as a candidate. Or keep the window and note the remaining case next to the extraction criterion.

### R3-8 — feasibility (minor)

**Problem**

> In M1 the anchor is itself parsed from pasted text (the Sent line of a header block), and the rule has no fail-closed case for it. Outlook writes the Sent line in a regional format, and some of those formats are numeric (general product knowledge, not verified in this review). A numeric Sent line read in the wrong order gives a wrong anchor and can give a wrong year. Example: 'Sent: 12/01/2026' means 12 January 2026 and is read as 1 December 2026, and the quote is 'by January 30'. The first January 30 on or after 2026-12-01 is 2027-01-30, which is 31 + 29 = 60 days later and inside the window, so the field is filled with 2027-01-30. The intended date is 2026-01-30.

**Remedy**

> Treat a Sent line that is missing, cannot be parsed, or is in an ambiguous numeric format as no anchor, the same way an ambiguous candidate date is treated.

### R3-9 — clarity (minor)

**Problem**

> The removed sentence was the only statement of how `person` values are normalized and kept unique. The new text does not say that `login_id` and `email` are each unique, or that sign-in and the accepted-sender check ignore case. Mail addresses and directory usernames often arrive in mixed case, so a case-sensitive match would refuse a rostered user at sign-in or move the boss's forwarded mail to 'Not accepted'.

**Remedy**

> State that `login_id` and `email` are stored lowercased, are each unique, and are compared without regard to case.

### R3-10 — completeness (minor)

**Problem**

> Triage types each `login_id` by hand, but the value is whatever the proxy or the provider presents, and Open Question 14 checks it for the boss and one manager only. The design gives Triage no way to learn or check the value for anyone else. A person whose `login_id` was typed wrong is simply refused ('A signed-in user with no active row gets no access'), with nothing that shows what value arrived.

**Remedy**

> Show the presented sign-in value on the no-access page, or record refused sign-ins where Triage can see them, so a roster entry can be corrected.

### R3-11 — feasibility (minor)

**Problem**

> Under OIDC, sign-in is matched on 'the provider's username claim', and the claim is not named. In Entra ID the usual username claim (`preferred_username`) can change, and Microsoft advises against using it for authorization decisions. The stable identifiers are the subject and object ids (general product knowledge, not verified in this review). A renamed user would lose access, and a username reissued to another person would match the old row while it is still active.

**Remedy**

> Name the claim. Either match on the provider's immutable subject id, recorded at the person's first sign-in after one match on the username, or state why the username is acceptable here (single tenant, names assigned by an administrator) and that rows are deactivated when people leave.

### R3-12 — completeness (minor)

**Problem**

> The roll-up order for a multi-owner firedrill still lists three of the five assignment statuses ('blocked, then at risk, then on track'). `new` and `done` have no place in it. A freshly published firedrill where one of four owners has answered 'on track' and three are still `new` has no defined board status, and neither does a mix of done and not done.

**Remedy**

> Place `new` and `done` in the order (for example blocked, at risk, new, on track, with done ignored unless every assignment that has not ended is done).

### R3-13 — consistency (minor)

**Problem**

> An update posted by Lead or Triage for an owner 'answers the ask' and resets that assignment's staleness. The 70% criterion ('answered in the app'), the 80% freshness figure and the stop rule still do not say whether such updates count. If they do, all three can be met while managers keep replying by email and the boss or Triage types the answers in, which is the participation risk the stop rule exists to catch.

**Remedy**

> State that the three figures count only updates with an empty `on_behalf_of`, or report the on-behalf share next to them.

### R3-14 — clarity (minor)

**Problem**

> The Confirm step still says 'Owners are never filled unless the email names a person in the roster'. The roster also holds the boss and Triage, and the boss is named in nearly every chain he forwards, so the rule as written would prefill him as an owner. No step produces owners at all: the M1 rules cover title, requested-by, ticket refs and due date, and the M3 schema has no owner field. What counts as 'names' (full display name, first name, email address) is not said.

**Remedy**

> Either state that owners are always picked by hand, or add an owner rule to a named milestone: active managers only, matched on full display name or email address, and left empty when more than one person matches.

### R3-15 — consistency (minor)

**Problem**

> The API routes still post updates and update requests to a firedrill (`POST /api/firedrills/{id}/updates`, `POST /api/firedrills/{id}/update-requests`), while both belong to an assignment. A Lead asking one of four owners, or posting for an owner, has to name the assignment, and the routes do not show where.

**Remedy**

> Address the assignment in the route, or name the owner field in the request body.

### R3-16 — feasibility (minor)

**Problem**

> Moving unaccepted mail to a 'Not accepted' folder is a write to the mailbox. The M2 needs are still described as polling for new messages with access 'scoped to that one mailbox', and no permission is named. In Microsoft Graph, moving a message needs read and write access to mail, and read access alone does not allow it (general platform knowledge, not verified in this review). The M2 requests are filed in M0 and have the longest lead time, so a read-only request would have to be filed again.

**Remedy**

> Name the Graph application permissions the M2 request asks for (read and write on the intake mailbox for the move, and send if asks go out through Graph), or leave unaccepted mail in place and track it in the database.

### R3-17 — completeness (minor)

**Problem**

> The retention rules still cover rejected items and closed firedrills only. A draft that is saved and never published or rejected keeps its raw email indefinitely, and so does mail in the 'Not accepted' folder. The stored extraction result, which holds quotes from the email, is not mentioned in either rule.

**Remedy**

> Add a rule for drafts left unconfirmed (for example delete after 30 days) and for the 'Not accepted' folder, and say that the extraction result is deleted with the raw content.

### R3-18 — completeness (minor)

**Problem**

> No component performs the 90-day deletion. The only scheduled job in the Distribution Plan is the poller CronJob, which arrives in M2, and no milestone lists the purge.

**Remedy**

> Name the job that runs the deletion and the milestone it ships in.

### R3-19 — completeness (minor)

**Problem**

> Triage enters the roster by hand, but 'A signed-in user with no active row gets no access', so the first Triage row cannot be created through the app.

**Remedy**

> State how the first Triage person is seeded (for example a migration or a deploy-time setting).

### R3-20 — clarity (minor)

**Problem**

> Roles can come from two places ('group claims when OIDC supplies them, otherwise the `person` table'), and `person.role` exists either way. The design does not say which wins when a group claim and the roster disagree, or whether the fallback applies per deployment or per user (a user whose token carries no group list would need it). The mailbox poller has no token and can only read the roster, so someone who is a Lead by group claim but a manager in the roster would be allowed in the UI and refused as a sender.

**Remedy**

> Pick one role source per deployment, or state the precedence and that the poller always reads `person.role`.

### R3-21 — clarity (minor)

**Problem**

> The M2 ask sets Reply-To to 'the boss's address', but delegates with the Lead role and Triage can also request updates, so a reply to their ask goes to someone who did not send it. The From address of the app-sent ask is still not named.

**Remedy**

> Set Reply-To to the person who made the request (`update_request.requested_by`) and name the From address.

### R3-22 — feasibility (minor)

**Problem**

> The admission rule depends on the proxy flag `--openshift-sar`. The probe refused oauth-proxy again this round (it is not on the probe allowlist) and the repository holds only a one-line README, so the flag and its described effect remain unverified, not disproved.

**Remedy**

> Confirm the flag name and its argument format against the proxy's README or help output, and note that each roster person also needs a role binding in the project to pass it.

### R3-23 — feasibility (minor)

**Problem**

> Both effort figures are still unchanged ('about 2 hours', 'human: ~2 weeks / CC: ~1 day') although M0 gained the M1 requests and a conversation with a manager, and M1 gained the roster, per-owner assignments, the match-and-attach action, on-behalf updates and usage events. This round M1 also gained owner changes after publish, per-email sharing and a separate login identifier. For M0: 120 minutes minus 15 with the boss leaves 105 minutes for 10 requests (6 items in M1's 'Waits on' and 4 in M2's), about 10 minutes each before the manager conversation is counted.

**Remedy**

> Re-estimate M0 and M1 against the current scope, or say that the figures already allow for it.

### R3-24 — clarity (minor)

**Problem**

> 'Identical pasted text is flagged by a hash of the whitespace-normalized body', and 'flagged' is not defined. The design does not say whether an identical paste is refused or becomes a draft with a warning and a link to the earlier item. The earlier item may have been rejected, in which case only its hash remains.

**Remedy**

> State what the person sees and whether a draft is created, including when the earlier item was rejected.

### R3-25 — clarity (minor)

**Problem**

> The wireframe path 'projects/pcarpe4-situation-room/patrick-main-sketch-20261110-192633.png' is still relative to an unstated base. No file exists at that path under the repository checkout (checked again this round), and this review may not read outside the checkout, so the file's existence is unverified. The Screens section and the Assignment rely on it.

**Remedy**

> Give the absolute path or state the base directory, and confirm the file exists there.

### R3-26 — clarity (minor)

**Problem**

> References still lists 'Standard connectors with a Send an HTTP request action', which on its face conflicts with Premise 4's statement that on a standard license 'a flow cannot POST to an outside API'. The body never uses or explains the reference.

**Remedy**

> Add one sentence to Premise 4 saying what those actions can and cannot reach, or remove the reference.

### R3-27 — consistency (minor)

**Problem**

> Premise 1's success test is still that the boss 'stops asking managers for status by email', but the M1 ask is an email he sends from Outlook through a mailto link. Success Criteria use the narrower 'outside the app'.

**Remedy**

> Reword Premise 1 to match: he stops asking for status outside the board.

### R3-28 — clarity (minor)

**Problem**

> 'Draft' still has no entity. The sharing choice now has a field (`intake_item.shared_with_owners`), and the stale rule no longer reads a publish time, so those two parts are settled. What remains: 'save draft' has nowhere to store the reviewer's edits and picked owners other than an unspecified 'extraction result'. `intake_item.state` (new, drafted, published, rejected) has no failed value although the board shows intake failures, and no value for an item attached to an existing firedrill. `requested_by` still means the email's requester on `firedrill` and the person asking on `update_request`.

**Remedy**

> State that a draft is the intake item and where its edited fields and picked owners live (or add a draft entity), add failed and attached states, and rename one of the two `requested_by` fields.

### R3-29 — feasibility (minor)

**Problem**

> The oauth-proxy guidance still says 'run a fixed version' without naming one and 'accept the header only from the sidecar' without saying how. The two CVE identifiers cannot be checked with the tools this review is limited to. The probe for the proxy's flags was refused again (oauth-proxy is not on the probe allowlist), so `skip-auth-regex` stays unverified, not disproved.

**Remedy**

> Name the fixed release or image tag from the advisories, state the mechanism (for example the app listens only on localhost inside the pod and the Service exposes only the proxy port), and confirm the flag name against the proxy's own help output or README.

### R3-30 — clarity (minor)

**Problem**

> An M1 ask is still recorded on click whether or not the email is sent. The new owner-change rule cancels a pending ask only when its assignment ends. The design still gives the person no way to cancel an ask or to ask again while one is pending. Unsent asks therefore count as unanswered in the criterion 'at least 70% of asks are answered in the app within one working day', which is measured during M1.

**Remedy**

> Note the bias next to that criterion, or add a cancel-ask action and say whether Request update can be used again while an ask is pending.

### R3-31 — scope (minor)

**Problem**

> Request update M2 still 'can post to Teams', but no mechanism, permission or dependency for Teams appears in the M2 row, Dependencies or Open Questions, and it is not needed for the success test.

**Remedy**

> Remove the Teams mention, or name the mechanism and add its approval to the M2 dependencies.

### R3-32 — consistency (minor)

**Problem**

> The M2 dependency lists still disagree. 'Waits on' includes the SMTP relay and Dependencies 'For M2' does not, and Dependencies names a shared mailbox that 'Waits on' omits. The request to the Exchange admin to restrict delivery to the mailbox appears in neither list.

**Remedy**

> Make the M2 row and the Dependencies entry list the same items, including the delivery restriction.

### R3-33 — consistency (minor)

**Problem**

> Premise 6 still describes the cheap version as 'a Microsoft List plus a standard flow' (one flow), while Approach A says 'two standard flows'. The flows are still not described. Approach A is the dated fallback ('ship A for the boss'), so it has to be buildable from this description.

**Remedy**

> Use one count and say in a clause what each flow does.

### R3-34 — consistency (minor)

**Problem**

> The M3 model schema still returns `action_required` and `affected_systems`, which have no field in `firedrill` and no place on the confirm screen.

**Remedy**

> Add them to the data model and the confirm screen, or drop them from the schema.

### R3-35 — consistency (minor)

**Problem**

> Premise 2, the board's empty state and the accepted-senders rule all have the boss start a firedrill by pasting or forwarding, but the Lead role's permissions (read everything, request updates, confirm drafts, reassign, close, post an update for an owner) still do not include creating intake.

**Remedy**

> Add 'start a firedrill (paste, upload or forward)' to the Lead role.

### R3-36 — completeness (minor)

**Problem**

> The board has a 'due this week' count, but nothing says how a firedrill past its due date is shown or counted, or where firedrills with no due date fall in the 'soonest due' sort.

**Remedy**

> State whether overdue items are included in 'due this week' or flagged separately, and where items without a due date sort.

### R3-37 — clarity (minor)

**Problem**

> The freshness target is at least 80% and the stop rule fires below half (50%). A result from 50% up to but not including 80% misses the target without triggering the stop rule, and no outcome is stated for that band.

**Remedy**

> Say what happens in that band (for example continue to M2 but investigate first), or state that only the stop rule gates further work.

### Prior finding evidence

> The Status rule is unchanged and still orders only 'blocked, then at risk, then on track'.

**R2-7 → R3-13 (persisting)**

> The on-behalf rule still 'answers the ask', and neither the new Active rule nor the Success Criteria say whether updates with `on_behalf_of` set count toward the three figures.

**R2-8 → R3-14 (persisting)**

> The Confirm step still reads 'Owners are never filled unless the email names a person in the roster', and no rule or schema field produces an owner.

**R2-9 → R3-15 (persisting)**

> The API section is unchanged: updates and update requests are still posted to `/api/firedrills/{id}/...` with no assignment or owner named.

**R2-10 → R3-16 (persisting)**

> The Accepted senders paragraph still moves mail to a 'Not accepted' folder, and the M2 row and Dependencies still name no Graph permission.

**R2-11 → R3-17 (persisting)**

> The two retention bullets are unchanged and still cover only rejected items and closed firedrills.

**R2-12 → R3-18 (persisting)**

> No purge job appears in State and operations, the Milestones table or the Distribution Plan.

**R2-13 → R3-19 (persisting)**

> The `person` line was rewritten but still says only 'In M1 Triage enters the boss and the managers by hand. A signed-in user with no active row gets no access.' Nothing says how the first Triage row is created.

**R2-14 → R3-20 (persisting)**

> 'Where roles come from: group claims when OIDC supplies them, otherwise the `person` table' is unchanged, with no precedence and no statement about the poller.

**R2-15 → R3-21 (persisting)**

> The M2 ask line still says 'Its Reply-To is the boss's address' and names no From address.

**R2-16 → R3-22 (unverified)**

> The `--openshift-sar` sentence is unchanged. The probe for oauth-proxy was refused again this round (OFFICE_HOURS_PROBE_REFUSED, not on the allowlist), and the repository holds only a one-line README, so there is no source to check the flag against.

**R2-17 → R3-23 (persisting)**

> The Milestones table still shows 'about 2 hours' for M0 and 'human: ~2 weeks / CC: ~1 day' for M1, while this round added owner changes, per-email sharing and `login_id` to M1.

**R2-18 → R3-24 (persisting)**

> The Duplicates paragraph is unchanged: 'Identical pasted text is flagged by a hash of the whitespace-normalized body', with no statement of what flagged means.

**R2-19 → R3-25 (persisting)**

> The Wireframe line is unchanged and still gives a relative path with no base. Reading that path under the repository checkout returned 'File does not exist'.

**R2-20 → R3-26 (persisting)**

> The 'Send an HTTP request' reference and Premise 4 are both unchanged, and the body still does not explain the reference.

**R2-21 → R3-27 (persisting)**

> Premise 1 still reads 'stops asking managers for status by email' while the M1 ask is a mailto email.

**R2-22 → R3-28 (persisting)**

> Two parts are settled: the sharing choice is stored in `intake_item.shared_with_owners`, and the stale rule now reads the assignment's own creation time. The rest is unmet: a draft still has no entity or stated home for edits and picked owners, `intake_item.state` is still (new, drafted, published, rejected) with no failed or attached value, and `requested_by` still has two meanings.

**R2-23 → R3-29 (persisting)**

> The guidance 'run a fixed version, avoid `skip-auth-regex`, and accept the header only from the sidecar' is unchanged. No version or mechanism is named, and the probe for oauth-proxy was refused again.

**R2-24 → R3-30 (persisting)**

> 'The app records the ask when the button is clicked' is unchanged. The only cancellation added is the automatic one when an assignment ends. There is still no cancel or re-ask action, and the 70% criterion carries no note about unsent asks.

**R2-25 → R3-31 (persisting)**

> The M2 ask line still says 'and can post to Teams', with no mechanism or dependency listed.

**R2-26 → R3-32 (persisting)**

> M2 'Waits on' still lists the SMTP relay that Dependencies omits, Dependencies still lists the shared mailbox that 'Waits on' omits, and the delivery restriction is in neither.

**R2-27 → R3-33 (persisting)**

> Premise 6 still says 'a standard flow' and Approach A still says 'two standard flows', and neither describes them.

**R2-28 → R3-34 (persisting)**

> The model schema line is unchanged, and `firedrill` (rewritten this round) still has no `action_required` or `affected_systems` field.

> The Lead role line is unchanged and still has no permission to start a firedrill.

**R2-30 → R3-36 (persisting)**

> The Board description still has a 'due this week' count and a 'soonest due' sort with no rule for overdue items or items without a due date.

**R2-31 → R3-37 (persisting)**

## CEO Review

This section and every section after it, up to the amendments list at the very end of the plan, is the record of the interactive review of 2026-11-10 and 2026-11-11 as it stood when that review closed. It is history. The plan body above was amended again by /autoplan on 2026-11-11, and where the two differ the body and the amendments list govern. Edits made after the author's document approval (D14) are approved, or not, at the /autoplan final gate.

Run by /plan-ceo-review on 2026-11-10. Review depth: strategy-only, because the author picked this review to pressure-test scope and the right-sized bet, not to design the implementation. Rows are at capability level, with "implementation owner must prove" notes where detail is owed. Working plan: this file. Mirror: docs/designs/situation-room.md.

### 0A. Premise challenge

- **Real problem.** The boss has no shared record of open firedrills, so he rebuilds the picture from his inbox and asks managers one at a time.
- **Target outcome.** He answers "what is open, who has it, is it moving" in under a minute and stops asking for status outside the board.
- **Do-nothing cost.** Not measured. How many firedrills are open, how often he asks and how many get dropped are unknown (docs/designs/situation-room.md:28).
- **Direct or proxy.** "One place to see status" is solved directly. "Stop chasing" is solved only in part: in M1 the ask is still an email he sends, and it becomes automatic no earlier than M2.
- **Largest unproven premise.** Demand rests on one unquoted statement, and no manager has been asked (docs/designs/situation-room.md:19).

### 0B. Existing leverage and reality rows

The repo holds a one-line README and this design, so there is no code to reuse. Leverage comes from the platform: cluster login or the org identity provider for sign-in, OpenShift Routes for TLS, Python's standard `email` package for message parsing, Postgres from the org's offering, and Microsoft Graph for mailbox pull. Nothing is rebuilt that a platform feature already provides.

Premise table:

| # | Claim | Receipt | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|

| P2 | No shared record has been tried | docs/designs/situation-room.md:26 | unverified: author's statement |
| P3 | A standard license cannot use the HTTP action or custom connectors | docs/designs/situation-room.md:53 | verified in docs for connector tiers (Microsoft Learn license types), HTTP action classification from Microsoft Q&A and community posts, not tested in the tenant |
| P4 | An internal OpenShift route is unreachable from Microsoft's cloud | docs/designs/situation-room.md:53 | unverified: cluster location unknown |

| P6 | oauth-proxy passes X-Forwarded-User and X-Forwarded-Email, has `--openshift-sar` and `-skip-auth-regex`, and documents no groups header | docs/designs/situation-room.md:163 | verified by a static read of the openshift/oauth-proxy README on 2026-11-10 |
| P7 | Exchange RBAC for Applications can scope an app to one mailbox | docs/designs/situation-room.md:111 | verified in docs (Microsoft Learn and admin guides), not tested |

### 0C. Dream state

```
CURRENT STATE                THIS PLAN                       12-MONTH IDEAL
Inbox plus memory.           One board. A person forwards    Firedrills arrive from every
He asks managers one         or pastes an email, confirms    source with no forwarding
at a time. Nothing           a draft, picks owners.          (mail, ServiceNow, chat).
shared, nothing        --->  He asks from the board,   --->  Status arrives without anyone
measured.                    managers answer on one          asking. He gets a Monday
                             screen. Staleness is            digest. His peers and his own
                             visible.                        boss read the same board.
                                                             The record shows which sources
                                                             create firedrills and how long
                                                             they take to close.
```

The plan moves toward the ideal. Assignments, updates and asks are the right records for it, and intake is already a set of interchangeable paths. What the plan does not yet reach: status without asking, sources beyond email, and any reader beyond the one boss.

### 0E. Mode

Mode: SCOPE EXPANSION, chosen by the author in answer to D2 of this review on 2026-11-10. The review had recommended SCOPE REDUCTION (about 40 planned files [estimated] against a 15-file threshold). The author's choice governs. No new approach decision was needed.

### 0F and 0G. Expansion framing

**10x check.** The board runs the loop. In the approved plan the boss still does the asking and managers still have to adopt a new screen. In the 10x version the board asks when something turns stale, managers answer by replying to the email, what they need from him surfaces at the top of the board, and on Monday the whole picture arrives in his inbox. That is roughly twice the build for a tool he never has to remember to use.

**Platonic ideal.** He forwards an email and forgets about it. A draft appears with every field backed by a quote, he picks owners in two clicks, and from then on status arrives without anyone asking. Each manager answers in one sentence from wherever they already are. When a manager is stuck on him, he sees it within the hour. Nothing on the board is ever older than he has agreed to tolerate, and when it is, the board has already asked. After a quarter he can show his own boss where the firedrills come from.

**Delight scan.** Five adjacent touches of about twenty minutes each: four status links in the ask email (L1), ticket numbers that link to ServiceNow (L2), an overdue badge and count (L3), a "waiting until a date" pause on the stale clock (L4), and a "seen by" mark on updates (L5).

**Outside this review's proposals.** Pulling ticket state from ServiceNow, intake from sources other than email, and readers beyond the one boss belong to the 12-month ideal and are not proposed here.

### Scope dispositions

Each proposal was decided by the author on 2026-11-10, one question at a time. The ledger above holds each answer and its exact scope.

**Accepted into this plan (9 at this point in the review. OPS, accepted later under Section 8, makes 10).** The plan body above was amended the same day to carry them, in Screens, Data model, Intake, Extraction, Updates and asks, Login and roles, State and operations, Milestones, Open Questions 15 and 16, Success Criteria, Distribution Plan and Dependencies.

| ID | Addition | Milestone |
|---|---|---|
| X1 | Managers answer an app-sent ask by replying to the email | M2 |
| X2 | The board sends one ask itself when an assignment turns stale | M2 |
| X3 | Monday 7:00 digest to the boss | M2 |
| X4 | "Needs you" list at the top of the board, with a Handled action | M1 |
| L1 | Four status links in the ask | M1 |
| L2 | Ticket refs link to ServiceNow | M1 |
| L3 | Overdue badge and count, undated firedrills sort last | M1 |
| L4 | "Waiting until a date" pauses the stale clock | M1 |
| L5 | "Seen by" mark on updates | M1 |

**Deferred to TODOS.md (1).** X5, the monthly "where firedrills come from" page. Reason: it needs a month or two of data that does not exist yet. Revisit after M1 has run for a month.

**NOT in scope.**

- X5, the monthly sources page: deferred, see above.
- Pulling ticket state from ServiceNow, intake from sources other than email, and readers beyond the one boss: not proposed in this review.
- An intake endpoint for other systems (`POST /api/intake`): no caller today, per the approved design.

### Spec review round 1

An independent reviewer read this plan and the CEO scope summary together on 2026-11-10 and scored them 6 of 10, with 31 findings: 9 on completeness, 7 on consistency, 7 on clarity, 2 on scope and 6 on feasibility. It found the two documents in agreement on every accepted item. The gaps were inside this plan.

- **One finding was a choice** and went to the author: when M2 work starts relative to the four-week M1 check (ledger row ORDER).
- **The rest were applied as mechanics the accepted additions need**, with no change to what was approved. Automatic mail is never posted or answered. Every send has a recorded state, retries and a failure banner. The sender is the app's shared mailbox. A stale period and the one-ask rule are defined. Status words in replies must match exactly. Raw replies are kept for 30 days. Waiting is left out of the freshness figures. The time zone and day boundaries are stated. The manager's own view of the board is described. The Assignment gained the consent questions the additions depend on.
- **Not changed, recorded as open.** "Can post to Teams" in the M2 ask still has no mechanism or dependency. Removing it is a scope cut the author has not been asked about.

Earlier reviewer concerns in the office-hours list above that these amendments settle: R3-4 (an ended assignment accepts no update), R3-9 (case and uniqueness of email and login), R3-15 (handlers address an assignment), R3-16 (mailbox read and write is requested), R3-17 and R3-18 (retention for drafts, and a purge job), R3-21 (sender and Reply-To of app-sent mail), R3-23 (effort re-estimated), R3-25 (wireframe path), R3-36 (overdue and undated firedrills). R3-13 and R3-30 are settled in part. That list is generated and is left as written, so it is out of date for these entries. The /autoplan amendments of 2026-11-11 settle more of them: R3-2 and R3-12 (status roll-up), R3-3 and R3-35 (the Lead role), R3-10 (refused sign-ins are shown), R3-19 (the first Triage person), R3-28 (where a draft lives, intake states, the ask's field name), R3-30 (an ask can be cancelled), R3-31 (Teams) and R3-33 (Approach A's flows). Its other entries remain open and are minor.

Question: D3 — X1: Let managers answer an ask by replying to the email?
Project/branch/task: situation-room on main, expansion proposal 1 of 10 for the approved design.
ELI10: Today a manager has to click a link, sign in and fill in a form. With this, they hit Reply, type a sentence or two, and the board shows it as their update. The app reads replies from its own mailbox, strips the quoted thread, and sends back a one-line receipt with a link to fix anything. It moves reply-to-update from "later" into the mailbox milestone (M2).
Stakes if we pick wrong: Without it, the board depends on managers adopting a new screen, which is the plan's biggest risk. With it, a reply the app misreads can sit on the board as a wrong update until someone notices.
Recommendation: Add because replying is the habit your managers already have, and this removes the reason the stop rule exists.
Note: options differ in kind, not coverage — no completeness score.
Pros / cons: listed under each option.
Net: about a week of extra work in M2 so that updating costs one reply.
Header: X1 reply
A) Add to this plan's scope (recommended)
Ships in M2 with mailbox pull. Effort M (human: ~1 week / CC: ~half a day). Risk medium. Reuses the M2 mailbox poller and the update record. Covered by tests on reply parsing, sender matching and the receipt.
✅ Managers update from the inbox they already live in
✅ Removes the adoption risk the stop rule was written for
❌ A reply the app misreads posts a wrong update for a while
B) Defer to TODOS.md
Recorded as a TODO with this context and left out of this plan. Effort S (zero implementation work now). Risk low. Reuses nothing yet. Nothing to test until it is picked up.
✅ M2 stays the size the design doc describes today
✅ You can decide after seeing whether managers use the form
❌ The first weeks depend on managers opening a new screen
C) Skip
Not in scope and no TODO. Effort S (zero implementation work). Risk low. Reuses nothing. Nothing to test.
✅ One less moving part: updates only ever come from the form
✅ No chance of a misread reply landing on the board
❌ Reply-by-email is dropped from the later list as well

Question: D10 — L3: Flag overdue firedrills and give them their own count?
Project/branch/task: situation-room on main, expansion proposal 8 of 10 for the approved design.
ELI10: The board counts what is due this week but says nothing once a date has passed. With this, a past-due firedrill shows "OVERDUE 3d", overdue gets its own count at the top, and firedrills with no due date sort after the dated ones.
Stakes if we pick wrong: Without it, an overdue item looks the same as one due next month. With it, there is one more count across the top of the board.
Recommendation: Add because overdue is the first thing he will look for, and it settles an open reviewer concern about sort order.

Question: D11 — L4: Let "waiting until a date" pause the stale clock?
Project/branch/task: situation-room on main, expansion proposal 9 of 10 for the approved design.
ELI10: Some firedrills cannot move until a known date, such as a vendor call next Wednesday. Today those turn stale and get asked about anyway. With this, the owner or your boss sets "waiting until Nov 24" with a reason, the row shows it, and the assignment is not stale until that date passes.
Stakes if we pick wrong: Without it, the stale count includes items nobody can move, and he learns to ignore it. With it, "waiting" can be used to dodge asks unless he reads the reasons.
Recommendation: Add because a stale count only helps if every stale row is one somebody could act on.

Automatic asks and the digest after the check |

Question: D13 — ORDER: Does milestone 2 wait for the four-week check on milestone 1?
Project/branch/task: situation-room on main, a sequencing gap the spec reviewer found in the amended plan.
ELI10: The plan has a stop rule: if, four weeks after the first milestone goes live, fewer than half of the active assignments have a recent update, stop adding features and go talk to the managers. You then added three mail features to milestone 2. The plan does not say whether building them waits for that four-week check. Reply-to-update is the feature meant to fix the very problem the check measures, which is managers not updating.
Stakes if we pick wrong: Wait, and the fix for low adoption arrives a month or more after adoption has already failed in front of your boss. Don't wait, and you may build three features on a board nobody is using.
Recommendation: Start M2 when its approvals land because reply-to-update is the remedy for the risk the check measures, so holding it back until the check fails gets the order backwards.
Note: options differ in kind, not coverage — no completeness score.
Pros / cons: listed under each option.
Net: build the adoption fix early at the risk of wasted work, or wait for proof and risk losing your boss's attention.
Header: ORDER
A) Start M2 when approvals land (recommended)
M2 work begins as soon as its approvals arrive, without waiting for the four-week check. The stop rule pauses work after M2 and never pauses reply-to-update. Effort S (zero implementation work, sequencing only). Risk medium. Reuses the plan as amended. Covered by the same weekly figures.
✅ The fix for low adoption is ready when adoption is tested
✅ Approval wait time is used for building, not for idling
❌ Three features may get built on a board nobody opens
B) Wait for the four-week check
No M2 work until M1 has been live four weeks and more than half of active assignments are fresh. Effort S (zero implementation work, sequencing only). Risk medium. Reuses the plan as amended. Covered by the stop rule as written.
✅ Nothing more is built until the board has proven itself
✅ The stop rule keeps its plain meaning with no exceptions
❌ Managers get no reply-by-email during the make-or-break month
C) Split it
Mailbox pull and reply-to-update start when approvals land. Automatic asks and the Monday digest wait for the check. Effort S (zero implementation work, sequencing only). Risk low. Reuses the plan as amended. Covered by the stop rule for the two held items.
✅ The adoption fix ships early and the extras wait for proof
✅ Mail in his name only starts once the board is trusted
❌ Two delivery rules to track inside a single milestone

## CEO Review: Step 0 close

### 0H. Document approval

The author approved these versions of the working plan and the CEO scope summary on 2026-11-11 (answer to D14: "Approve, fixes not re-verified"). The spec review ran one round: 6 of 10, 31 findings. Edits were applied for the 31, 26 in full and 5 in part, and each was checked for presence in the text. The count includes the Teams finding, which was recorded as open with its line left unchanged. That is a check of applied edits, not a verdict. Decision recorded: spec not re-verified. CEO summary: ~/.gstack/projects/pcarpe4-situation-room/ceo-plans/2026-11-10-situation-room.md.

### 0I. Temporal interrogation

```
HOUR 1 (foundations)    Where the code lives (OQ 8). Which login path, and what value it hands the app
                        (OQ 3, 11, 14). Which database, CI and registry (OQ 6). The app's time zone.
                        How the first Triage person gets a roster row, which the plan does not say (R3-19).
HOUR 2-3 (core logic)   Status roll-up when owners are a mix of new and done (R3-12). Where a saved
                        draft's edits live (R3-28). Working-day arithmetic for stale, waiting, overdue.
HOUR 4-5 (integration)  Whether a mailto body with four links survives the boss's Outlook. What identity
                        the proxy or the provider presents. In M2: reply matching (OQ 16), reply stripping,
                        sending as the shared mailbox, recognizing automatic mail.
HOUR 6+ (polish/tests)  Real emails and real replies as fixtures. Tests that move the clock across a
                        weekend. Tests that run each scheduled job twice. Every banner state.
```

No feasibility blocker needs a new decision from the author today. Each has an open question, an owner and a fallback. Effort, all estimated: M0 about half a day. M1 human ~3 weeks / CC ~2 days. M2 human ~3-4 weeks / CC ~2 days. M3 human ~1 week / CC ~half a day.

## CEO Review: Sections 1 to 11

Strategy-only depth. "Owner" means the implementation owner, who is the author unless stated. "Owner must prove" marks detail this review does not decide.

### Section 1. Architecture

```
            Microsoft 365 (tenant)                            OpenShift project
 +------------------------------------------+      +--------------------------------------------+
 | Boss's Outlook --forward--> Shared       |      |  Route (TLS)                               |
 |      |                      mailbox      |      |     |                                      |
 |      | mailto ask (M1)        ^   |      |      |  login: OIDC in the app, or oauth-proxy    |
 |      v                 replies|   |pull  |      |     |                                      |
 | Managers' Outlook ------------+   +------+----->|  Web app x2 (pages, no JSON in M1)         |
 |      ^   asks, receipts, digest (M2)     |<-----+     |                                      |
 |      +-----------------------------------+ Graph|  Postgres <--- CronJobs: purge (M1, daily) |
 | Identity provider (Entra ID or AD) <-----+------+               poller (M2, every 5 min)     |
 +------------------------------------------+ OIDC |               scheduler (M2, 7:00)         |
  Model endpoint (M3, only if approved) <----------+--------------------------------------------+
  ServiceNow: link-out only (L2). No data flows to or from it.
```

Data flows, four paths each:

```
INTAKE   happy  paste or forward -> intake_item -> rules -> draft -> person confirms -> firedrill + assignments
         nil    no subject, no header blocks -> draft with empty title and requester, person types them
         empty  empty body -> refused at the form (M1), not accepted in the mailbox (M2), nothing stored
         error  mailbox or Graph unreachable -> mail waits in the mailbox, nothing lost, banner on the board
UPDATE   happy  ask -> form or reply -> update -> assignment status, ask answered, stale clock reset
         nil    reply with no reference -> not accepted, the manager gets a notice
         empty  reply with no usable text -> nothing posted, notice
         error  send fails -> retried, then mail_send failed, row shows "Ask failed", banner
```

State machines:

```
intake_item  new -> drafted -> published          attach also ends here; the plan has no "attached" state (R3-28)
                        |-> rejected (raw deleted)
                        |-> deleted after 30 days unconfirmed
             invalid: published -> drafted, rejected -> anything. What prevents them: owner must prove.
assignment   new -> on_track | at_risk | blocked | done        any -> ended (owner removed or replaced)
             done -> another status by a later update: the plan does not forbid reopening. Owner must decide.
             waiting is a dated flag beside the status, not a status.
firedrill    open -> closed (Lead or Triage only). closed -> open is not described. Owner must decide.
mail_send    pending -> sent | failed (after 3 tries). A failed ask gets a new mail_send on the next run.
```

Findings:

- **WARNING, coupling.** Reply-to-update, automatic asks and the digest all hang on one shared mailbox and one app registration. That is justified (one grant to request), but it is a single point of failure for all of M2. The M1 paths keep working without it.
- **WARNING, single points of failure.** One maintainer. One Postgres (the board is down when it is). One mailbox grant.
- **OK, scaling.** Expected size is tens of firedrills and under 20 people. At 10x nothing breaks. At 100x the board's per-row lookups and the one-email-per-assignment rule hurt first.
- **OK, security boundaries.** Every handler sits behind login plus an active roster row, with a role check per action. Mailbox access is scoped to one mailbox.
- **Production failure per integration.** Identity provider down: nobody signs in, and the owner must prove a clear error page. Graph down: intake and sends wait, banner. Relay refuses: retries, then "Ask failed". Model endpoint down (M3): the draft falls back to rules only.
- **Rollback posture.** M1 is a new app with nothing before it. Rollback is redeploying the previous image, minutes once CI exists (OQ 6). That holds only if migrations stay backward compatible. Owner must prove.
- **What would make it obvious to a new engineer (expansion).** One rule: everything is an event on an assignment. Updates, asks, waits, needs, seen marks and replies all attach to one assignment and read as one timeline. M4's history then costs almost nothing.
- **Platform piece (expansion).** `mail_send` plus the scheduler is what later features reuse.
- **Artifact value test.** The plan adds no report or matrix about the work itself. `usage_event` and the Friday figures are product measurements with named readers (the author and the boss) and a decision they drive (the stop rule).

Issues: 2 warnings. No new decision.

### Section 2. Error and rescue map

Capability rows. Exception classes are not named at this depth.

| Capability | What can go wrong | Rescued? | Rescue action | User sees |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Paste intake | Empty or oversized paste | Y | Refused at the form. Owner must set the size limit | Inline message |
| Rules extraction | No headers, ambiguous date, no ticket | Y | Field left empty, candidates listed | Empty, marked field |
| Confirm a draft | Two people confirm the same draft | unknown | Owner must prove one wins and the other is told | unknown |
| Sign-in | Provider down, or no roster row | partly | No access without a row | Owner must prove a clear page, not a raw error |
| Board | Database unavailable | unknown | Owner must prove | Owner must prove an error page, not an empty board |
| Mailbox pull (M2) | Graph down, token expired, grant removed | Y | Mail waits, retried on the next run | Intake banner |
| Reply-to-update (M2) | Automatic mail, no reference, wrong sender, bad strip | Y | Not posted, notice sent, raw reply kept 30 days | Receipt or notice |
| Sending (M2) | Relay refuses, Graph error | Y | Three retries, then failed | "Ask failed", banner |
| Scheduler (M2) | Misses 7:00, or runs twice | Y | Recorded before send, same-day catch-up for the digest | Late mail, never double |
| Purge (M1) | Job fails | Y | Runs again the next day | Banner |
| Model pass (M3) | Malformed output, empty output, refusal, invented quote | partly | Invented quote is rejected by the substring check. The other three are not named in the plan | Owner must prove each ends as an empty field |
| Settings | Wrong time zone or ServiceNow address | N | None | Wrong send times, dead links. Visible, not silent |

Error paths mapped: 12. Gaps: 4 unknown or partial (confirm conflict, sign-in page, database down, model failure modes) and 1 not rescued but visible (settings). No row is silent, so no critical gap. No new decision.

### Section 3. Security and threat model

| Threat | Likelihood | Impact | Mitigated? |
|---|---|---|---|
| Stored email holds credentials or sensitive content (password rotation chains are in scope) | Med | High | Partly. Lead and Triage only, per-email sharing, retention, OQ 13. Open until the org's data rules are known. The author owns this before any real email is stored |
| Work data on a personal GitHub remote | Med | High | Open, OQ 8. No real email or credential may be committed |
| A forged reply posts a false update for another manager | Low | Med | Partly. Reference plus sender match, delivery restriction. Owner must prove the check uses the authenticated sender, not the display From |
| Identity header spoofing through oauth-proxy | Low | High | Partly. Fixed version, no `skip-auth-regex`. Owner must prove the app port is unreachable except through the proxy |
| A manager opens another firedrill's original email by changing an id | Med | Med | Rule exists. Owner must prove every handler checks role and ownership on the server |
| Script or markup in pasted email or reply text runs on the board | Med | Med | Not stated. Owner must prove all email-derived text is escaped on output |
| Mail loop or flood from the app | Low | Med | Yes. Automatic mail ignored, app mail marked auto-generated, receipts and notices carry no matchable reference, five notices per address per day, one automatic ask per stale period |
| Email text steering the model (M3) | Med | Low | Yes. A field needs a verbatim quote and a person confirms |
| Leaked database, OIDC or Graph secret | Low | High | Yes for storage (cluster Secrets, never in the repo). Rotation is not described. Owner must state it |
| Sensitive action with no trail | Low | Med | Yes for writes (`audit_log`). Reads of original emails are not logged. Owner must decide |

Issues: 10, of which 4 are High impact (2 open, 2 partly mitigated). All four already have an owner and an open question. No new decision.

### Section 4. Data flow and interaction edge cases

```
INPUT           VALIDATION               TRANSFORM              PERSIST                OUTPUT
paste / mail -> size, sender, ---------> normalize, rules ----> intake_item, draft --> confirm screen
                automatic-mail check     (model in M3)
  nil / empty   refused / not accepted   empty fields marked    nothing stored         message or notice
  too long      size limit (owner must set the number)
  duplicate     message id, body hash    possible match shown   attach, nothing new    "attach" action
  encoding      owner must prove non-ASCII subjects and bodies survive
  timeout       pull retried next run; model call falls back to rules
reply (M2) ---> reference + sender ----> strip quote, status -> update (message id) -> board + receipt
  duplicate     same message twice -> one update (source_message_id)
```

Async ordering. Invariant: an assignment gets an automatic ask only while it is stale and has no unanswered ask, at most one per stale period.

```
 scheduler (7:00)                 manager's update or reply      assignment
 reads "stale, no open ask"                                      stale, no ask
                                  update is posted               fresh, stale period over
 writes ask, sends mail                                          fresh AND auto-asked   <- breaks the invariant
```

With the other order (update first) the scheduler reads "fresh" and sends nothing. Nothing in the plan prevents the first order. The same check-then-write gap exists between a Lead's manual ask and the scheduler, which can produce two asks. Owner must prove the staleness check and the ask are one transaction (or hold a lock on the assignment), with a test that pauses the scheduler between its read and its write and lets an update land.

| Interaction | Edge case | Handled? | How |
|---|---|---|---|
| Request update | Double-click | unknown | Owner must prove one ask is recorded |
| Request update (M1) | Clicked, mail never sent | Y, accepted | Shows as asked. Known bias in the 70% figure |
| Update form | Submitted twice, or back button | unknown | Owner must prove one update |
| Update form | Assignment ended while the form was open | Y | Refused, shows who has it now |
| Confirm a draft | Left open for days while a matching firedrill appears | unknown | Owner must prove the match is rechecked at publish |
| Handled | Two Leads click | unknown | Owner must prove the first wins and the second sees it handled |
| Board | No firedrills | Y | Empty state |
| Board | Hundreds of rows | unknown | No paging stated. Fine at the expected size |
| Scheduler | Backlog after an outage | Y | Asks only for what is stale now. Digest catches up the same day |
| Poller | Hundreds of messages waiting | unknown | Owner must set a per-run cap |

Edge cases mapped: 10. Unhandled or unknown: 6, plus the ordering gap above. No new decision: each is verification owed, not a choice between behaviors.

### Section 5. Code quality

No code exists, so this section reviews structure the plan implies.

- **WARNING, possible over-build.** A `setting` table with an in-app settings screen for six values and one maintainer. Deploy-time configuration would do for M1. Owner decides.
- **WARNING, under-specified.** A saved draft has no stated home for edits and picked owners (R3-28). The status roll-up for a mix of new and done owners is undefined (R3-12).
- **Naming.** `requested_by` means the email's requester on `firedrill` and the asker on `update_request` (R3-28).
- **Where complexity will collect.** The stale, waiting and overdue date rules. They belong in one pure function driven by a table of cases.
- **One source of truth.** "May this assignment be asked now" is read by the button, the scheduler and the digest. It should be decided in one place.

Issues: 4. No new decision.

### Section 6. Tests

No test exists and no test method was chosen for the plan as a whole, so these are the checks each accepted behavior needs, not an approved test plan. `no_claim` says what a green result would not prove.

| New thing | Test type | Happy path | Failure path | Edge case | no_claim |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Date rules: stale, waiting, overdue, working days | Unit | Monday update, N=3, stale Friday morning | n/a | Weekend, the waiting date itself, time zone boundary | none beyond protects |
| Extraction rules | Unit on fixtures | One absolute date is filled | Ambiguous date stays empty | SCTASK is not read as TASK. Year-less date with no anchor stays empty | real Outlook formatting (fixtures are pasted samples) |
| Confirm to publish | Integration | Draft becomes a firedrill with owners | No owner is refused | Match offered, attach creates nothing new | two people confirming at once |
| Roles and access | Integration | Manager updates own assignment | Manager updates another's: refused | Manager requests an unshared original email by id: refused | identity provider behavior (login is mocked) |
| Update form and status links | System | Link opens with its status chosen | Ended assignment is refused | Double submit gives one update | the boss's Outlook handling of the mailto body (manual proof in M0) |
| Needs, Handled, Seen, Waiting | Integration | Each change shows to the right role | n/a | A need closes when its assignment ends | none beyond protects |
| Reply-to-update (M2) | Integration on real reply fixtures | Reply becomes an update and a receipt | Automatic mail posts nothing. Wrong sender gets a notice | "Done with the call, still waiting" is not read as done. Same message twice gives one update | Graph and Exchange behavior (mocked, needs one live check on the tenant) |
| Automatic asks (M2) | Integration with a controlled clock | One ask at 7:00 on the stale day | Send fails: "Ask failed", asked again | An update lands between the scheduler's read and write. The job runs twice | mail delivery (sender is mocked) |
| Monday digest (M2) | Integration | Four lists for a fixed data set | n/a | All four empty gives the all-clear. A missed run | mail delivery (sender is mocked) |
| Purge | Integration | Each retention rule applied | A failed run shows in the banner | Nothing to purge | none beyond protects |
| Model pass (M3) | Unit with recorded responses | Quote-backed fields kept | Malformed, empty, refusal and invented quote each end empty | A date that does not match its quote is dropped | live model behavior (responses are recorded) |

- **The test for shipping at 2am on a Friday.** The whole loop on fixtures with the clock controlled: paste, confirm, ask, update, board shows fresh. In M2: stale, automatic ask, reply, fresh.
- **The hostile QA test.** An out-of-office reply from the owner. A reply whose first line is "Done?". A manager editing the address to reach another assignment.
- **The chaos test.** Kill the scheduler between recording a send and sending it. Run it twice. Drop the database in the middle of a confirm.
- **Pyramid.** Mostly unit (date rules, extraction), fewer integration, a handful of system tests.
- **Flakiness.** Everything time-based has to take an injected clock. No test may depend on the real mailbox.
- **Load.** None needed at the expected size.
- **Model evaluation (M3).** The repo has no CLAUDE.md, so no eval suite is defined. The real-email test set is the evaluation, and the rules-only result is the baseline.

Gaps: 3 checks that no automated test can supply and that are owed before the feature they guard ships: the live tenant check for reply matching (OQ 16), the mailto proof in the boss's Outlook (M0), and the ordering tests named in Section 4.

### Section 7. Performance

- **OK at the expected size.** Tens of firedrills, under 20 people.
- **WARNING, lookups per row.** Each board row needs its latest update, open ask, open need and seen mark per assignment. Owner must fetch each kind once for the page, not once per row.
- **Indexes owed.** `update` by assignment and time. `update_request` by assignment and answered. `intake_item` by content hash and by message id. `mail_send` by state. `assignment` by owner and ended.
- **Jobs.** The poller needs a per-run cap. The scheduler's worst case is one email per stale assignment, which is tens. The purge is trivial.
- **Slowest paths.** The confirm screen on a very long chain. The model call in M3, which takes seconds and belongs at intake, not at page load. The board with every row shown.
- **WARNING, connections.** Two app replicas and three jobs share one small Postgres. Owner must size the pool.

Issues: 2 warnings. No new decision.

### Section 8. Observability

- **Signals that it works.** Intake items per day, drafts waiting to be confirmed, `mail_send` by state, last success time of the poller, scheduler and purge, and the Friday figures.
- **Logging.** One structured line per intake item, rule outcome, ask, send and reply, carrying ids and never the email body. Owner must prove.
- **Debuggability.** `audit_log`, `mail_send` and `update.source_message_id` are enough to reconstruct why a row shows as answered. The reason a message was not accepted is not recorded per message. Owner must record it.
- **GAP, alerts.** The plan shows failures as a banner on the board. Nothing reaches the maintainer when nobody is looking at the board.
- **GAP, admin view.** There is no page showing job last-run times, failed sends or not-accepted mail.
- **GAP, runbooks.** None are planned for an expired mailbox grant, a relay that rejects mail, a restore from backup, or a person who cannot sign in.

Gaps: 4 (reason not recorded, alerts, admin view, runbooks). Alerts, an admin view and runbooks are launch scope for a system that sends mail in someone's name, and adding them is new scope, so they go to the author as ledger row OPS.

No alerts, no health view, no runbooks | Adds a Health page for Triage, an alert to the maintainer on each job or send failure, and four short runbooks | TODOS.md, not in this plan | unchanged |

Question: D15 — OPS: Add a small operations slice so failures reach you without opening the board?
Project/branch/task: situation-room on main, Section 8 (observability) of the CEO review.
ELI10: Today the plan shows problems as a banner on the board. If nobody is looking at the board, nobody finds out that mail stopped flowing or a job failed. This adds three things: a Health page for you showing when each job last ran, failed sends, and rejected mail with the reason; an alert to you when the poller, a send, the scheduler or the purge fails (through the cluster's alerting, or by email once the app can send); and four short runbooks for an expired mailbox grant, a relay that rejects mail, a restore from backup, and a person who cannot sign in.
Stakes if we pick wrong: Without it, the first time intake stalls quietly your boss notices before you do. With it, each milestone grows by a couple of hours of work.
Recommendation: Add because you are the only person who can fix this app, so you should be the first to know when it breaks.
Note: options differ in kind, not coverage — no completeness score.
Pros / cons: listed under each option.
Net: about two hours of build so that failures find you before they find your boss.

The Health page, alerts and runbooks ship with the milestone whose parts they cover: M1 for sign-in, database and purge, M2 for mail. Effort S (human: ~3 days / CC: ~2 hours). Risk low. Reuses mail_send, the job records and the banner. Covered by tests that each failure raises its alert and shows on the page.
✅ You hear about a stalled poller before your boss does
✅ Failed sends and rejected mail are listed with reasons
❌ One more page and four documents to keep up to date
B) Defer to TODOS.md
Recorded as a TODO with this context and left out of this plan. Effort S (zero implementation work now). Risk medium. Reuses nothing yet. Nothing to test until it is picked up.

❌ A failure outside working hours sits until someone looks

## CEO Review: Sections 9 to 11

Section 8 decision applied: the author added the operations slice (ledger row OPS, D15). Section 8 now has 1 open gap: the reason a message was not accepted must be recorded per message, which the Health page needs. Owner must prove.

### Section 9. Deployment and rollout

```
DEPLOY   build image -> push to registry -> pre-deploy Job runs migrations -> roll Deployment (2 replicas)
         -> smoke: health endpoint, database connect, board renders read-only
         -> by hand: sign in as each role, publish a test firedrill, post an update, banner is clear
         -> first hour: purge ran, and in M2 the poller succeeded and no send failed

ROLLBACK is the app broken after a deploy?
           yes -> redeploy the previous image (minutes)
                    did this deploy change the schema?
                      no  -> done
                      yes -> the old image must run on the new schema (additive migrations only)
                             if it cannot: restore from backup (proved once before launch)
           a mail feature misbehaves (M2) -> global switch off, the M1 paths keep working
```

- **Migrations.** Additive within a milestone, run before the new image. Owner must prove the previous image runs on the new schema, since two replicas roll one at a time.
- **WARNING, switches.** Automatic asks have a global switch. Reply-to-update and the digest have none, so turning either off needs a deploy. Owner decides whether each gets a switch.
- **WARNING, no staging.** The plan names one OpenShift project. M2 mail must first be tried against a test mailbox, never the real one. Owner must say whether a second project exists.
- **Deploy-time window.** A scheduler run can overlap a rollout. Recording each send before it goes out covers that.
- **Open dependency.** CI, registry and code location are still open (OQ 6, OQ 8), and "deployed" in M1 waits on them.
- **What would make shipping routine (expansion).** One pipeline that builds, migrates, deploys and runs the smoke check on every merge, so a deploy is a merge.

Risks flagged: 3 (switches, staging, open CI and registry). No new decision.

### Section 10. Long-term trajectory

- **Debt taken on.** Operational: one person owns uptime, mail, backups and the roster. Testing: the tenant checks are manual. Documentation: about 25 minor concerns from the first review are open and the README is one line. Design: the wireframe predates nine additions.
- **Path dependency.** Intake and replies are tied to one Microsoft 365 mailbox, which fits this org. The primary Lead is a single setting, so one deployment serves one boss. A second Lead with their own board would need that to become per-Lead.
- **Knowledge concentration.** High. This document is the only description of the system.
- **Reversibility: 4 of 5.** Data sits in Postgres and can be exported, the mail features switch off, and the Microsoft List fallback stays open.
- **Ecosystem fit.** The repo has no conventions yet. The stack is a proposal.
- **The one-year question.** Yes, provided the "everything is an event on an assignment" rule is written at the top of the code.
- **What comes after (expansion).** The sources page (X5, deferred), ticket state from ServiceNow, boards for other Leads, intake from chat. Assignments, `mail_send` and the scheduler support all four. Only "boards for other Leads" needs a model change.

Debt items: 4. No new decision.

### Section 11. Design and UX

```
BOSS     digest or habit -> Board: "Needs you" -> counts -> rows (stale first)
                               |            |
                               |            +-> Request update -> "Asked" -> answer arrives -> row is fresh
                               +-> Handled -> the manager sees it
         forward or paste -> Confirm a draft -> pick owners -> Publish -> row appears
MANAGER  ask email -> status link -> sign in -> Update screen (status chosen) -> Send -> "Seen by" later
         ask email -> Reply (M2) -> receipt quoting what was posted -> link to correct it
         board -> Update on own row -> same Update screen
```

- **What he sees first, second, third.** What needs him, then the counts, then the rows with stale at the top. That order is right for him. Managers get the same board, so their own assignments are not first. Owner decides whether managers open on "mine".
- **State coverage.**

| Feature | Loading | Empty | Error | Success | Partial |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Board | not stated | stated | banner | stated | several owners with mixed status: roll-up incomplete (R3-12) |
| Needs you | not stated | not stated | not stated | Handled shown | n/a |
| Confirm a draft | not stated | empty fields marked | not stated | publish | candidates listed |
| Manager update | not stated | n/a | ended assignment message | what the form shows after Send is not stated | n/a |
| Ask (M1 mailto) | n/a | n/a | no mail program on the device: not stated | "Asked" | never sent, accepted |

- **Emotional arc.** For the boss: relief at one place, then trust because stale is honest (waiting and overdue are shown apart), then habit through the digest. For a manager: mild annoyance at a new ask, then low effort (a link or a reply), then a payoff (seen, handled). The risk is "Needs you" turning into a public count of how slow the boss is, which is why Open Question 15 asks him first.
- **Generic-UI risk.** Moderate. Counts over a table is what every tracker looks like. What is specific here is staleness as the main signal, quotes as evidence on the draft, and "Needs you". Those three should dominate.
- **Design system.** The repo has no DESIGN.md.
- **WARNING, phone width.** Managers will open the update link from email on a phone, and the plan never mentions small screens. The update screen has to work at phone width. Owner must prove.
- **Accessibility.** Stale and overdue must not rely on color, and the wireframe already uses text badges. The status choice and Send must be reachable by keyboard.
- **What would make it feel inevitable (expansion).** For a manager the update screen is the whole product: one screen, no navigation. L1 to L5 are the small touches, and all five are accepted. No further proposals.

Issues: 4 (loading and error states unstated, post-send state unstated, phone width, managers' first view). No new decision. Recommended before implementation: /plan-design-review on this plan. After implementation: /design-review on the running app.

## CEO Review: Outside voice

Outside coverage: none. Codex is not installed, so no outside model reviewed this plan. The bounded fallback ran instead: a read-only reviewer on this same harness, given the CEO summary and the first 30KB of the plan. That excerpt ends before Milestones, Open Questions, Success Criteria and the Assignment. The reviewer finished in about two minutes with 20 findings and `VERDICT: FINDINGS`. It is a source of findings, not outside coverage.

| # | Severity | Finding | Disposition |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Critical | No evidence gate is left anywhere in the plan | Partly a false premise: the Assignment, which the reviewer could not see, says "before any code". The real gap is that no consequence is stated. Taken to the author as ledger row GATE |
| 2 | High | The Microsoft List should run from day one, not as a week-three fallback | Not reopened. This is office-hours Approach C, which the author declined (D11). The reviewer brings an argument, not new evidence. The disagreement stays on record |
| 3 | High | M1 puts the intake burden on the boss | Factual correction applied: in M1 the boss forwards to the author, who pastes and confirms, as the author first described. Intake now says so |
| 4 | High | Reply-to-update and the on-screen incentives work against each other | Stands as a known tension. A reply cannot raise a need or set a wait, and its author sees "Seen by" and "Handled" only on the board. Owner: the author, watching the M2 figures |
| 5 | High | Success is measured by board visits, which the digest and replies reduce | Partly covered in text the reviewer could not see: the visit target is for M1 and is reviewed when the digest ships, and week four asks the boss directly. Premise 1 still says "by email" (earlier concern R3-27, open) |
| 6 | High | The estimate counts typing, and a security review of an app that stores email is the likely blocker | Applied as an unknown with an owner: Open Question 18 and an M1 dependency. The effort figures already say they exclude approvals |
| 7 | Medium | Replies are published to every manager with no warning | Applied: the app-sent ask says a reply is posted to the board where the other managers can read it |
| 8 | Medium | The receipt cap breaks the safeguard that replaced confirmation | Applied: receipts are never capped. The cap covers notices only |
| 9 | Medium | Status parsing will rarely fire | Applied in part: the receipt states the status as it stands and repeats the four status links. The exact-match rule stays |
| 10 | Medium | Retries and catch-up have nothing to run them, and sends are not once-only | Applied: the five-minute job sends and retries, the 7:00 job only creates rows, an automatic ask is unique per assignment and stale period, and one duplicate after a crash is accepted |
| 11 | Medium | Reply matching omits In-Reply-To and the conversation id | Applied: named in Open Question 16 as a third candidate, not verified |
| 12 | Medium | "Seen by" records a board load, not a read | Not changed: the author approved "once your boss has had that update on his screen" (D12). One correction applied: seen marks are not written to the audit log |
| 13 | Medium | One global stale threshold, and a wait with no cap | Not changed. The misuse risk of waiting was stated when the author accepted L4 (D11). A threshold per firedrill would be new scope. Owner: the author, after a month of data |
| 14 | Medium | Peer visibility is assumed harmless | Applied: Open Question 2 now asks the managers too |
| 15 | Medium | Status is entered twice for ticketed work | Not changed. Pulling ticket state is in the 12-month ideal and was not proposed |
| 16 | Medium | A reply from a delegate is dropped | Stands as a limitation: only the assignment's owner can reply. A delegate uses the link, or the owner is changed |
| 17 | Low | Graph message ids change when mail moves folders | Owner must prove a stable id is used before relying on it for duplicates |
| 18 | Low | The CronJob time zone field and M1 alerting both depend on the cluster | Applied in part: Open Question 19 covers alerting. Owner must prove the time zone field on the cluster version in use |
| 19 | Low | "Answered" is defined twice | Applied: the stored link is the only definition |
| 20 | Low | One time zone for every manager | Accepted limitation for one site. Owner must revisit if managers sit in other zones |

The reviewer's recommendation was to gate on the Assignment, stand up the Microsoft List on day one, and hold X1 to X3 until the List has two to four weeks of data. Only the gate is taken to the author. The other two would reverse D11, D3 to D5 and D13 without new evidence, so they are recorded as the reviewer's standing disagreement with the plan.

Question: D16 — GATE: Make the conversation with your boss a written gate before milestone 1?
Project/branch/task: situation-room on main, the outside reviewer's top finding on the amended plan.
ELI10: The plan tells you to talk to your boss "before any code", but it does not say what happens if his answers do not match the plan. The outside reviewer's main point is that everything added today rests on things nobody has checked, starting with "he asked for this". This writes the gate down: no milestone 1 work starts until that conversation has happened, and if he does not confirm he wants one place for status that his managers update, you stop and rethink before building anything.
Stakes if we pick wrong: Without it, weeks of work can start on a guess. With it, a lukewarm answer from him pauses the project until you have rethought it.
Recommendation: Apply because the conversation costs about an hour and is the only check left on the six unverified claims.
Note: options differ in kind, not coverage — no completeness score.
Pros / cons: listed under each option.
Net: one hour with your boss decides whether the next several weeks are spent on the right thing.
Header: GATE
A) Apply this change (recommended)
The plan states that M1 starts only after the Assignment, and that a "no" or a shrug from the boss stops work until the approach is revisited. Effort S (zero implementation work, a rule in the plan). Risk low. Reuses the Assignment as written. Covered by the Assignment's own notes.
✅ Nothing gets built on "he asked for this" until he says it
✅ Turns an hour of conversation into a real go or no-go
❌ A lukewarm answer pauses work you were ready to start
B) Keep this row's current value
The Assignment stays "before any code" with no stated consequence. Effort S (zero implementation work). Risk medium. Reuses the plan as it is. Nothing further to verify.
✅ You keep the freedom to start building whatever he says
✅ The plan stays exactly as you approved it an hour ago
❌ Weeks of work can still begin on an unchecked claim
C) Investigate before choosing
Leave it undecided until you have thought about what answer from him would change your mind. Effort S (zero implementation work). Risk medium. Reuses nothing. Nothing to verify yet.
✅ You decide the threshold yourself before writing it down
✅ No rule goes into the plan that you might not follow
❌ The gap stays open in the final report of this review
D) Defer this proposed change only
Record the proposal as open and move on. Effort S (zero implementation work). Risk medium. Reuses nothing. Nothing to verify yet.
