#### Sections 1 to 11, this pass

Strategy depth, as before. The plan above already holds the full diagrams, registries and tables from the review of 2026-11-10. Each section below says what this pass examined and what changed.

**Section 1, architecture.** Examined the system diagram and data flows against the amended body. The shape is unchanged: one web app, Postgres, three scheduled jobs. Four records were added (`job_run`, `refused_sign_in`, `inbound_message`, `weekly_figure`) and the five-minute job now pulls mail, sends mail and makes up a missed 7:00 run. WARNING: that job is now the single point for all of M2's mail, and the 7:00 scheduler does little it could not do. Whether to fold the scheduler into it is left to the engineering phase. Issues: 1.

```
 Microsoft 365                                   OpenShift project
 +----------------------------------+           +---------------------------------------------+
 | Boss, managers (Outlook)         |  sign-in  | Route (TLS) -> web app x2 (pages, handlers) |
 | Shared mailbox                   |---------->|                  |                          |
 |   Inbox -> Processed             |           |               Postgres                      |
 |         -> Not accepted          |<--Graph-->| five-minute job (M2): pull Inbox, record    |
 |         -> Automatic             |           |   inbound_message, move, send mail_send,    |
 | Identity provider                |           |   make up a missed 7:00 run from 7:30       |
 +----------------------------------+           | scheduler (M2, 7:00): create ask and digest |
                                                |   rows only                                 |
                                                | purge (M1, 6:00): retention, mailbox copies,|
                                                |   Friday figures                            |
                                                +---------------------------------------------+
```

```
ask        open (pending or sent, or an M1 mailto ask) -> answered (any update on the assignment)
                |-> cancelled (assignment ends, firedrill closes, Lead or Triage cancels)
                |-> unsent (latest send failed or expired) -> open again (automatic: new send at next 7:00
                                                              while still stale; clicked: only by asking again)
mail_send  pending -> sent | failed (3 tries) | cancelled (no longer applies) | expired (past expires_at)
firedrill  open -> closed -> open (Reopen, provisional): stale clocks restart, ended asks and needs stay ended
```

**Section 2, error and rescue.** Examined the 12 capability rows against the amendments. Changed rows: Sending (every kind of mail expires, a bounce counts as a failed send, cancelled when it no longer applies), Scheduler (made up once from 7:30), Sign-in (the no-access page shows the presented value), Settings (the time zone is no longer a setting, so the row narrows to the ServiceNow address). New rows: a failed Friday figures run (made up late, shown as late) and Reopen with no active owner (refused until one is picked). Rows: 14. Gaps: 3, all carried forward unchanged (two people confirming one draft, the page shown when the database is down, the model's failure modes in M3). No row is silent.

**Section 3, security.** Examined the 10 threats. The two open High rows now have an answer due before any code: where the code lives (Open Question 8) and whether email copies may be stored (Open Question 13), with a named fallback design. The forged-reply row now has a proof with a consequence: if the authenticated sender cannot be read, mailbox intake and reply-to-update do not ship. One new surface was checked: anyone can put the subject marker on a message to have it ignored, which is harmless because ignoring is the safe outcome. Role changes now come from directory claims at sign-in, which removes a stale hand-set role. New issues: 0. High rows still open until answered: 2.

**Section 4, data flow and edge cases.** Examined the ordering gap and the 10 interaction rows. The ordering gap is closed in the body: the sender rechecks an automatic ask before each attempt, and one update answers every ask in one transaction. Settled rows: asking again (always a new ask), reopening, deactivating, a reply to the app's own mail. Still unknown and owed by the engineering phase: a double-click on Request update, a double submit of the update form, two people confirming one draft, and the poller's per-run cap. Unknown: 4.

**Section 5, code quality.** No code exists, so this examined the structure the plan implies. Fixed: the two meanings of `requested_by`, and where a draft lives. Still a WARNING: the `setting` table now holds nine values for one maintainer. New WARNING: the ask has four derived states (open, answered, cancelled, unsent) computed from two records, which is where bugs will collect, so it wants one function and a table of cases, like the date rules. Issues: 2.

**Section 6, tests.** Examined whether each accepted behavior has a check. Every item in the amendments list names one, which gives the engineering phase about 40 checks to turn into a test plan. They are checks, not an approved plan. Owed and not automatable: the mailto proof in the boss's Outlook, the sign-in value, and the seven tenant proofs for M2. Gaps: those 9 proofs.

**Section 7, performance.** Examined the new records and jobs for cost. Nothing changes at the expected size (tens of firedrills, under 20 people). The weekly figures job and the per-message record are trivial. The two earlier warnings stand (per-row lookups on the board, connection pool sizing). New issues: 0.

**Section 8, observability.** Examined whether OPS now has data behind it. It does: job runs, refused sign-ins, inbound mail outcomes and the weekly figures each have a record, and the Health page is specified per milestone. One conditional gap remains and is now stated in the plan: without the cluster's alerting, a failing five-minute job or failing sends reach the maintainer only on the Health page. Gaps: 1.

**Section 9, deployment.** Examined switches, staging and the job schedule. All three mail features now have a switch. The CronJob time-zone dependency has a question and a fallback. The migration Job creates the first Triage person. Still open: no second project or test mailbox is named for trying M2 mail, and CI, registry and code location are unanswered. Risks: 2.

**Section 10, trajectory.** Reversibility stays 4 of 5. Debt added by this pass: the plan file is now over 2000 lines because it carries its own review history, and should be split into a plan and a record once approved. Debt reduced: the single-maintainer risk now has a question (a second Triage person, a named owner). The reviewer's sharpest point stands on its own: the most valuable next hour is the conversation with the boss, not more planning. Debt items: 5.

**Section 11, design.** UI scope is present. This pass added states the screens must show: "Ask failed", "receipt not delivered", "automatic reply received", the banner's audience and dismiss action, the no-access page with the presented value, and the Closed filter. Still unstated: loading and error states for "Needs you" and the confirm screen, what the form shows after Send, phone width, and what a manager sees first. Those four go to the design phase, which runs next. Issues: 4, carried.

#### Required outputs, this pass

**Reality rows.** The seven `REALITY:` lines are under 0B of this record. Check result is recorded at the phase close.

**NOT in scope.** Deferred: the sources page (X5, the author's answer D7) and Teams posting (provisional, in TODOS.md). Held for the author and not applied: running the Microsoft List first (decision 80), gating each M2 feature on a Milestone 1 signal (decision 83), and deferring the JSON form of the handlers (decision 77). Not adopted: the reviewer's three reframes, an assignment notice email, Lead-only defaults for visibility, a written kill condition, and a stale threshold per firedrill.

**What already exists.** Unchanged: no code, and the plan reuses platform features without rebuilding any. Added: a two-hour check, before requests are filed, of what ServiceNow dashboards, Planner and Lists already give the boss.

**Dream state delta.** Unchanged. This pass did not move the product toward the 12-month ideal. It made the approved scope specific enough to build and added two checks on whether it should be built.

**Error and rescue registry, failure modes registry.** The tables in the plan above stand, with the row changes listed under Section 2 of this pass: 14 rows, 0 critical gaps, 3 rows with unknowns for the engineering phase.

**Scope decisions.** No new addition was proposed. One small feature moved from M4 to M1 (the Closed filter with Reopen) and is provisional.

**Diagrams.** Two redrawn in this pass (system architecture, and the ask, mail and firedrill states). The other five in the plan above still hold.

Done assignments are left out of that roll-up unless every assignment that has not ended is done."},{"oldText":"When every assignment is done, the board shows the firedrill as \"Done, ready to close\" and it stays open until closed. Done assignments are never stale and cannot be asked.","newText":"When every assignment that has not ended is done, the board shows the firedrill as \"Done, ready to close\" and it stays open until closed. Done assignments are never stale and cannot be asked. A done assignment becomes active again only when its owner, a Lead or Triage posts an update on the form with another status. An email reply never reopens one (see Reply-to-update). Closing a firedrill cancels its open asks and closes its open needs. A Lead or Triage can reopen a closed firedrill from the board's Closed filter, which lists closed firedrills newest first. Reopening clears `closed_at`, brings the assignments back with the status they had, and starts each one's stale clock at the reopen date. Asks and needs that the close ended stay ended. An assignment whose owner is no longer active is ended at reopen, and if that would leave the firedrill with no assignment, Reopen requires picking an active owner first. Owners can be changed only while a firedrill is open. Email content already deleted by the retention rules is not restored."},{"oldText":"Counts across the top double as filters: open, stale, asked with no reply, waiting, drafts to confirm, due this week, overdue.","newText":"Counts across the top double as filters: open, stale, asked with no reply, waiting, drafts to confirm, due this week, overdue. Open, due this week and overdue count firedrills. Stale, asked with no reply and waiting count assignments, and each of those filters shows the firedrills that have at least one such assignment. Drafts to confirm counts intake items. A Closed filter, with no count, lists closed firedrills newest first with a Reopen action for a Lead or Triage."},{"oldText":"- `intake_item`: source (paste, eml, mailbox), raw content, content hash, external message id, received_at, state (new, drafted, published, rejected), extraction result, firedrill (set on publish or attach, so one firedrill can hold several emails), shared_with_owners (per email, off by default)","newText":"- `intake_item`: source (paste, eml, mailbox), raw content, content hash, external message id, received_at, state (new, drafted, published, attached, rejected, failed), extraction result, draft_fields, firedrill (set on publish or attach, so one firedrill can hold several emails), shared_with_owners (per email, off by default). A draft is the intake item itself. `extraction result` keeps what the rules, and later the model, produced. `draft_fields` holds the person's working values and picked owners from \"save draft\" until publish. `attached` means the email was stored on an existing firedrill. `failed` means normalizing or extraction raised an error: the item shows in the intake banner and can be retried or rejected."},{"oldText":"- `update_request`: assignment, requested_by, requested_at, answered_by_update, origin (manual, auto), reply_ref (M2, the reference a reply is matched on), mail_send (M2, app-sent asks only)","newText":"- `update_request`: assignment, asked_by (the person who clicked, or the primary Lead for an automatic ask), requested_at, answered_by_update, cancelled_at, origin (manual, auto), clock_start (automatic asks only: the date the stale clock was counting from, which names the stale period), reply_ref (M2, the reference a reply is matched on), mail_send (M2, app-sent asks only, the latest send). `firedrill.requested_by` is the email's requester, a different thing, which is why this field is not called `requested_by`."},{"oldText":"- `mail_send` (M2): kind (ask, digest, receipt, notice), recipient, state (pending, sent, failed), attempts, created_at, sent_at. Every email the app sends has a row, written before the send.","newText":"- `mail_send` (M2): kind (ask, digest, receipt, notice, alert), recipient, about (the ask, update, inbound message or digest date it concerns, or the job or send an alert is about), reason (notices only), state (pending, sent, failed, cancelled, expired), attempts, created_at, last_attempt_at, expires_at, sent_at. Every email the app sends has a row, written before the send. A digest is unique per Monday date. The text of an email is built when it is sent, from the row and current data, so a late send is never out of date."},{"oldText":"Holds the stale threshold N, the retention periods, the ServiceNow address, the time zone, the primary Lead (the person asks are sent for and the digest goes to), and the global automatic-asks switch. Triage changes settings in the app.","newText":"Holds the stale threshold N, the retention periods, the ServiceNow address, the primary Lead (the person asks are sent for and the digest goes to), the maintainer's alert address, the global automatic-asks switch and, from M2, the reply-to-update switch and the Monday digest switch. Triage changes settings in the app. The time zone is not a setting: it is deploy-time configuration read by the app and by every scheduled job, so day boundaries and the 7:00 run cannot disagree."},{"oldText":"This is the data source for the first success criterion.","newText":"This is the data source for the first success criterion.\n- `job_run`: job (purge, five-minute, scheduler), started_at, finished_at, outcome (ok, failed), detail. One row per run. The Health page and the alerts read it. When the five-minute job makes up a missed 7:00 run, that work is recorded as a `scheduler` run.\n- `refused_sign_in`: presented value, at. Kept 30 days.\n- `inbound_message` (M2): the message's stable id, sender, received_at, kind (intake, reply, automatic, other), outcome (drafted, posted, not accepted, ignored), reason, the intake item or update it produced, raw content (replies only, deleted after 30 days). Every message the poller handles gets one row, written before the message is moved, so no message is handled twice even if the move fails.\n- `weekly_figure`: week, name, value. The Friday figures under Success Criteria, stored each Friday by the daily job, so the stop rule reads a number that cannot drift afterwards."},{"oldText":"Setting or changing a wait is recorded as an update whose text is the reason, so it answers a pending ask.","newText":"Setting or changing a wait is recorded as an update whose text is the reason, and clearing one early is recorded as an update that says so. Either answers a pending ask. When a Lead or Triage does it for the owner, the update carries `on_behalf_of`, like any other update posted for an owner."},{"oldText":"Anything else is moved to a \"Not accepted\" folder in the mailbox and counted on the board's intake banner.","newText":"Anything else is moved to a \"Not accepted\" folder in the mailbox and listed on the Health page with its reason. The poller reads the Inbox only, and every message it handles leaves the Inbox: to \"Processed\" when it became a draft or an update, to \"Not accepted\" when it was refused for any reason (the sender, a missing reference, not the owner, an ended or done assignment, a closed firedrill, no usable text), and to \"Automatic\" for automatic mail and for replies to the app's own digests, receipts, notices and alerts. Each message gets an `inbound_message` row with its outcome and reason before it is moved."},{"oldText":"The same mailbox message is never processed twice (message id).","newText":"The same mailbox message is never processed twice: its `inbound_message` row is keyed on the message's stable id (the Internet Message-ID header, which should not change when a message moves folders, to be proven on the tenant)."},{"oldText":"and that manager gets a notice pointing to the update link, so nobody believes they answered when they did not.","newText":"and that manager gets a notice pointing to the board, where each of their assignments has an Update button, so nobody believes they answered when they did not. Digests, receipts, notices and alerts carry no ask reference. They set Reply-To to the app's mailbox and carry a fixed marker in the subject, and a message that carries that marker is never drafted, never posted and never answered: it is filed as `other`, ignored and moved to \"Automatic\", whoever sent it. Each of those mails says that replies to it are not read and gives the link to use. While the reply-to-update switch is off no reply is posted: a message that carries an ask's reference is filed as not accepted, and its sender, if an active roster member, gets a notice pointing to that assignment's update link."},{"oldText":"A raw email reply is deleted 30 days after it was posted as an update.","newText":"A raw email reply is deleted 30 days after it was posted as an update. The purge applies the same periods to the copies in the shared mailbox: \"Not accepted\" and \"Automatic\" mail after 30 days, a processed reply 30 days after it was posted, and a processed intake email when its intake item's raw content is deleted."},{"oldText":"the app sends the email itself through the internal SMTP relay or Graph, and can post to Teams. It is sent from","newText":"the app sends the email itself through the internal SMTP relay or Graph. It is sent from"},{"oldText":"If neither works on this mail system, reply-to-update does not ship and replies are handled as in M1.","newText":"If no candidate works on this mail system, reply-to-update does not ship. Reply-to-update also has a switch in settings, so it can be turned off after launch without a deploy. Whenever it is off, for either reason, an app-sent ask sets its Reply-To to the primary Lead, says that replies are not tracked and to use the links, and a reply is handled as in M1: a Lead or Triage posts it for the owner. A reply to an ask that was sent before the switch was turned off still reaches the app's mailbox and is handled as described under Replies."},{"oldText":"when the reply has no usable text, when the assignment has ended or is done, or when the firedrill is closed.","newText":"when the reply has no usable text, when the assignment has ended or is done, or when the firedrill is closed. A reply cannot be trusted to mean \"reopen\", so the notice for a done assignment links to the form, which is the only place a done assignment can be made active again."},{"oldText":"when an active assignment turns stale, the scheduler sends one ask for it at 7:00 that morning, for the primary Lead, and the row shows \"Auto-asked\". A stale period starts when the assignment turns stale and ends at its next update or wait. The board sends at most one automatic ask per stale period.","newText":"each 7:00 run on a working day creates one ask, for the primary Lead, for every assignment that meets all of these: it is active and stale that morning, it is not waiting, its firedrill's `auto_ask` is on, the global switch is on, it has no open ask, and no automatic ask exists yet for its current stale period. The row then shows \"Auto-asked\". An open ask is one that is unanswered, not cancelled, and whose latest send is pending or sent (an M1 mailto ask has no send and is open until answered or cancelled). An ask whose latest send failed or expired is unsent: its row shows \"Ask failed\", it is not open, and it is left out of the 70% figure. A stale period is named by the date its stale clock started counting (the newest update, the assignment's creation, the end of a wait or the firedrill's reopening), which `update_request.clock_start` records, and it ends at the next update or wait. The board sends at most one automatic ask per stale period. The rule is therefore \"stale now and not yet asked in this period\", not \"turned stale today\": an assignment missed by a failed run is asked on the next run. Turning the global switch on, or a firedrill's `auto_ask` back on, never causes a burst: an assignment that was already stale at that moment is not asked automatically for its current stale period. It stays on the board as stale, where a Lead can ask by hand. The same holds when N is lowered: an assignment made stale by the change is not asked automatically for its current stale period. Request update is always available on an active assignment and always creates a new ask, even while another ask is open, and one update answers them all."},{"oldText":"No automatic ask is sent when an unanswered ask already exists, while the assignment is waiting,","newText":"No automatic ask is sent when an open ask already exists, while the assignment is waiting,"},{"oldText":"auto_ask (on by default, a Lead can switch it off), created_by, created_at, closed_at","newText":"auto_ask (on by default, a Lead can switch it off), auto_ask_changed_at, created_by, created_at, closed_at"},{"oldText":"The five-minute job sends pending mail and retries a failed send up to three times over an hour, then marks it failed. The 7:00 scheduler only creates the rows. An automatic ask is unique per assignment and stale period in the database, so two runs cannot create two. A crash between sending and recording can still send one duplicate, which is accepted. A failed ask does not count as asked: its row shows \"Ask failed\" and the next scheduler run may ask again.","newText":"The five-minute job sends pending mail and retries a failed send up to three times over an hour, then marks it failed. Before each attempt it checks that the email still applies: an ask that has been answered or cancelled is not sent, and an automatic ask is also not sent when its assignment is no longer stale or is now waiting. In each case the send is marked cancelled, and an automatic ask that no longer applies is cancelled with it. Every kind of mail has an `expires_at`: 24 hours after creation for an ask, a receipt, a notice and an alert, and the end of its Monday for a digest. Pending mail past its expiry is marked expired and shown on the Health page, whose retry action is offered only for mail that still applies, and never for an expired digest. The 7:00 scheduler only creates the rows. If no successful `scheduler` run is recorded for a working day by 7:30, the five-minute job does that work at its next run and records it as that day's `scheduler` run, so it happens once. An automatic ask is unique per assignment and `clock_start` in the database, and a digest is unique per Monday date, so two runs cannot create two. A crash between sending and recording can still send one duplicate, which is accepted. An ask whose latest send failed or expired is unsent: it is not open and does not count as asked. At the next 7:00 run an unsent automatic ask gets a new `mail_send` when its assignment is still active, stale and not waiting in the same stale period, both switches are on and it has no open ask. Nothing creates a second automatic ask for that period. An unsent ask that a person clicked is not retried: its row shows \"Ask failed\" and Request update works on it again."},{"oldText":"an ask whose `answered_by_update` is empty. That stored link is the only definition of answered.","newText":"an open ask, as defined under Automatic asks. An update answers every ask on its assignment that is unanswered and not cancelled, whatever the state of its send: each one's `answered_by_update` is set to that update in the same transaction. That stored link is the only definition of answered. An ask is cancelled, not answered, when its assignment ends, when its firedrill closes, or when a Lead or Triage cancels it from the row, for example an M1 ask whose email was never sent. Cancelled and unsent asks are left out of the 70% figure."},{"oldText":"A missed run is sent when the scheduler next runs that same Monday, and skipped after that.","newText":"If the 7:00 run fails, the five-minute job creates the digest at its next run after 7:30 that Monday (see Sending and failures). There is one digest per Monday, and one not sent by the end of its Monday is skipped. Triage can switch the digest off in settings."},{"oldText":"It sends automatic asks and, on Mondays, the digest. Each send is recorded before it goes out (see Sending and failures), so a job that runs twice does not send twice and a failed send is retried, not lost.","newText":"It creates the rows for automatic asks and, on Mondays, the digest, and the five-minute job sends them. Each send is recorded before it goes out (see Sending and failures), so a job that runs twice does not send twice and a failed send is retried, not lost. The 6:00 and 7:00 times rely on the cluster's CronJob time-zone field, which is asked about with the M0 requests. Without that field, each job runs hourly and the app's configured zone decides when the 6:00 and 7:00 work is due."},{"oldText":"The mailbox poller runs as a single CronJob on a short interval, five minutes by default, so two pods never process the same message.","newText":"The mailbox poller runs as a single CronJob on a short interval, five minutes by default, so two pods never process the same message. This is the \"five-minute job\": each run pulls the mailbox, sends pending mail, and makes up a missed 7:00 run."},{"oldText":"- A daily purge CronJob (M1) applies the retention rules.","newText":"- A daily purge CronJob (M1) runs at 6:00, applies the retention rules and, on Fridays, stores the week's figures in `weekly_figure`."},{"oldText":"A Health page for Triage shows when each job last ran, failed sends with a retry action, and not-accepted mail with the reason. An alert reaches the maintainer when the poller, a send, the scheduler or the purge fails, through the cluster's alerting or by email once the app can send.","newText":"A Health page for Triage shows when each job last ran and how it ended (`job_run`), failed and expired sends with a retry action, not-accepted mail with the reason (`inbound_message`), refused sign-ins with the value presented, and the Friday figures for the last eight weeks. In M1 it holds the purge, refused sign-ins and the figures. M2 adds the five-minute job, the scheduler, sends and not-accepted mail. An alert reaches the maintainer when the purge, the five-minute job, the scheduler or a send goes from working to failing, and once more when it recovers. A job that keeps failing does not alert again on every run. Sending is judged as a whole: it is failing when the latest send attempt failed, and recovered at the next success. Alerts go through the cluster's alerting where it is offered (Open Question 19), and the alerts depend on it: ask for it with the M0 requests. Without it, an alert about the purge or the scheduler goes by email to the maintainer's alert address once the app can send, delivered by the five-minute job. An alert about sending, or about the five-minute job itself, cannot be delivered by the app, because it would travel the path that failed: it shows on the Health page and the banner, and the runbook tells Triage to check that page each working day."},{"oldText":"| M0 | The Assignment, plus filing the requests for M1 and M2 | This week, about half a day of your time | Nothing |","newText":"| M0 | The Assignment first, then filing the requests for M1 and M2 | This week, about a day of your time | Nothing |"},{"oldText":"the Health page with alerts and runbooks for the M1 parts, deployed | human: ~3 weeks / CC: ~2 days | Namespace, database, login (OIDC client or cluster login), role source (AD groups or the roster), image build and registry, code location |","newText":"the Health page with alerts and runbooks for the M1 parts, the Closed filter with Reopen, deployed | human: ~3.5 weeks / CC: ~2.5 days | Namespace, database, login (OIDC client or cluster login), role source (AD groups or the roster), image build and registry, code location, and a security review sign-off if the org requires one (Open Question 18) |"},{"oldText":"| human: ~3-4 weeks / CC: ~2 days, not counting the proofs owed on reply matching, reply stripping and the sender address |","newText":"| human: ~4 weeks / CC: ~2.5 days, not counting the seven tenant proofs listed under Open Question 16 |"},{"oldText":"All effort figures are estimates. M0, M1 and M2 were re-estimated after the CEO review added scope on 2026-11-10.","newText":"All effort figures are estimates. M0, M1 and M2 were re-estimated on 2026-11-11 to include the operations slice and the amendments from the /autoplan review."},{"oldText":"If M1's prerequisites are not granted within three weeks of filing, ship Approach A for the boss and keep the requests open.","newText":"If M1's prerequisites are not granted within three weeks of filing, ship Approach A for the boss and keep the requests open. The security review, where one is required, is one of those prerequisites and counts toward the three weeks."},{"oldText":"File the M1 and M2 requests during M0. The M2 ones have the longest lead time.","newText":"File the M1 and M2 requests during M0, after the Assignment: a shared mailbox and the right to send mail are not requested for a tool the boss has not yet confirmed he wants. File them the same week, because the M2 ones have the longest lead time and the three-week fallback clock starts at filing. Before filing, spend two hours checking what the tenant already gives the boss (ServiceNow dashboards, Planner, Lists) and write down what each cannot do. At the visit, also ask him who the second Triage person will be, since intake stops when the only one is on leave, and whether this is sanctioned work with a named owner in the org."},{"oldText":"- **A) Microsoft List + two standard flows.** Not chosen by the author. It has no field extraction and is not a codebase the author owns.","newText":"- **A) Microsoft List + two standard flows.** One list with a row per firedrill (title, description, owner, due date, status, last update). One flow creates a row when the boss moves an email into a set Outlook folder, and the other reminds each owner on a schedule to update their rows. Not chosen by the author. It has no field extraction and is not a codebase the author owns."},{"oldText":"A Microsoft List plus a standard flow has never been tried here.","newText":"A Microsoft List plus two standard flows (Approach A) has never been tried here."},{"oldText":"If he declines either of the other two, \"Needs you\" is shown to Leads and Triage only, and the \"Seen by\" mark is not shown to managers.","newText":"If he says no to the first outright, automatic asks are not built in M2 and are recorded in TODOS.md. If he says not yet, they are built and stay off. Either way the ask a person clicks and the Monday digest are unaffected. If he declines the second, the \"Needs you\" list is shown to Leads and Triage only, and a manager still sees on their own row and update screen whether a need they raised was handled, by whom and when, and can still withdraw it. If he declines the third, \"Seen by\" is not built. His answers are known before M1 starts, because the Assignment is the gate."},{"oldText":"Matching the reply's In-Reply-To header, or Graph's conversation id, against the sent ask is a third candidate, not verified here.","newText":"Matching the reply's In-Reply-To header, or Graph's conversation id, against the sent ask is a third candidate, not verified here. Seven tenant proofs are owed before the M2 build, each with what happens if it fails. Reply matching: reply-to-update does not ship. Reply stripping: reply-to-update does not ship. Sending as the shared mailbox: app-sent asks, automatic asks and the digest are held, and mailbox intake can still ship. Recognizing automatic mail: reply-to-update does not ship. Marking the app's own mail as automatic (Graph may accept only custom headers on send, not verified here): send through the relay instead, and if that fails too, the notice cap and the check for automatic mail on the way in are the loop protection. Reading the authenticated sender and not the display From: mailbox intake and reply-to-update do not ship, because both trust the sender. A message id that survives a folder move: if none does, handled mail is not moved and is tracked only in `inbound_message`."},{"oldText":"Reply-to-update rests on that habit, and nobody has checked it. The Assignment asks one manager.","newText":"Reply-to-update rests on that habit, and nobody has checked it. The Assignment asks two or three managers. If they would rather click a link, ask every manager the same question before the reply-to-update work in M2 starts, and record the answers under Demand Evidence. If most prefer the link, the ORDER decision goes back to the author before that work starts."},{"oldText":"An update that a Lead or Triage posts for an owner does not count as an answer here.","newText":"An update that a Lead or Triage posts for an owner does not count as an answer here, and cancelled asks are left out. \"Within one working day\" means before the end of the next working day after the day the ask was sent, so an ask sent on a Friday is on time through Monday."},{"oldText":"The share of email replies that the manager then corrected is tracked as the misread rate.","newText":"The share of email replies that the manager then corrected is tracked as the misread rate, where a correction is a form update by the same owner within one working day of their email-reply update. Every Friday figure is computed by the daily job at its 6:00 run on Fridays, stored in `weekly_figure` and shown on the Health page. The freshness figure is a snapshot at that run. The ask figures cover asks whose one-working-day window closed in the seven days before the run, on assignments that were active when asked, leaving out cancelled and unsent asks. If the Friday run fails, the next successful run computes that week's figures and marks them late."},{"oldText":"Ask the manager one more: would you rather answer by replying to an email or by clicking a link?","newText":"Ask each manager one more: would you rather answer by replying to an email or by clicking a link? Ask him and them whether managers may see each other's firedrills (Open Question 2). While you are there, ask your boss what he has tried before (a list, a spreadsheet, a ServiceNow view) and note which Outlook he uses. If he tried a shared list and dropped it, write down why under Status Quo: Approach A then stops being the fallback unless that reason no longer applies, and another fallback is chosen before M1 starts. The visit checks the premises the plan leans on most: that he asked for this, that nothing shared has been tried, and how managers will answer. Three other unverified claims are checked elsewhere: which file type his Outlook saves (Open Question 12, noted at this visit), whether a route on the cluster can be reached from Microsoft's cloud (asked with the M0 requests, and the plan does not depend on the answer, because intake is pull either way), and what the two oauth-proxy advisories say (read them before choosing that login path)."},{"oldText":"These are examples of the handlers the UI itself calls, behind the same login. The list is illustrative, not complete.","newText":"These are examples of the handlers the UI itself calls, behind the same login. The JSON form is a second representation of the listed routes with no caller of its own today: the pages use the HTML form, and no outside tool can call the JSON form until service tokens exist. It is kept because the author asked for an API that other tools can call later. Whether to build the JSON form in M1 or add it with its first caller is a question for the engineering review and then the author. Until it is decided, the M1 scope and estimate include it. The list is illustrative, not complete."},{"oldText":"CronJobs for the poller and the scheduler, and a pre-deploy Job for database migrations.","newText":"CronJobs for the purge (M1), the poller and the scheduler (M2), alert rules for the cluster's alerting where it is offered, and a pre-deploy Job for database migrations, which also creates the first Triage person."},{"oldText":"request updates, confirm drafts, reassign, close, post an update for an owner, mark a need handled, set waiting, switch automatic asks off for a firedrill.","newText":"start a firedrill (paste, upload or forward), request updates and cancel an ask, confirm drafts, retry or reject a failed intake item, turn an email's sharing with owners on or off, add, remove or reassign owners, close and reopen, post an update for an owner, mark a need handled, set or clear waiting, switch automatic asks off or back on for a firedrill."},{"oldText":"  - Manager: read all open firedrills (published fields and updates), post updates and set waiting on their own assignments.","newText":"  - Manager: read all firedrills, open and closed (published fields and updates), and the original emails shared with them as an owner. On their own assignments: post updates, set or clear waiting, and raise or withdraw a need."},{"oldText":"  - Triage (the author): everything Lead can do, plus edit any field, manage the roster and change settings.","newText":"  - Triage (the author): everything Lead can do, plus edit any field, manage the roster and change settings, including the switches for automatic asks, reply-to-update and the Monday digest, and use the Health page with its retry and dismiss actions."},{"oldText":"- **Where roles come from:** group claims when OIDC supplies them, otherwise the `person` table. Either way the user must have an active `person` row.","newText":"- **Where roles come from:** the app, the poller and the seeding of the first Triage person all authorize on one field, `person.role`. When OIDC supplies group claims, each sign-in writes the mapped role into that field, so the directory stays the source and the claim wins over a value set by hand. A sign-in whose claims map to no role is refused. Without group claims, Triage sets the role by hand. Either way the user must have an active `person` row."},{"oldText":"If the first non-empty line is exactly one of the four status words, in any letter case and ignoring trailing punctuation, that becomes the status and the rest is the text.","newText":"If the first non-empty line is exactly one of the four status words, in any letter case and ignoring one trailing period, exclamation mark, comma or colon, that becomes the status and the rest is the text. A first line that ends in a question mark never matches."},{"oldText":"The manager gets a receipt that quotes exactly what was posted and links to the update screen, where a new update supersedes it.","newText":"The manager gets a receipt that quotes exactly what was posted and links to the update screen, where a new update supersedes it. If that receipt fails or expires, the update is flagged \"receipt not delivered\" on the board and on the Health page, so a Lead or Triage can check it with the owner."},{"oldText":"| M4 | History: a per-firedrill timeline of updates, asks and owner changes, and a list of closed firedrills | Later | M2 |","newText":"| M4 | History: a per-firedrill timeline of updates, asks and owner changes | Later | M2 |"},{"oldText":"Stale, fresh, asks and every figure in Success Criteria apply to active assignments only.","newText":"Stale, fresh and asks apply to active assignments only. The freshness figure and the stop rule judge \"active\" when the Friday figure is computed, and the ask figures judge it when the ask was made."},{"oldText":"2. May managers see each other's firedrills? The design assumes yes. Ask the managers as well as the boss: a status every peer can read may be written more defensively.","newText":"2. May managers see each other's firedrills? The design assumes yes. Ask the managers as well as the boss: a status every peer can read may be written more defensively. The Assignment asks it. If the answer is no, a manager's board shows only the firedrills they own, the \"Needs you\" list is shown to Leads and Triage only, and the M2 ask says a reply is visible to the boss and to that firedrill's other owners. Co-owners of one firedrill still see its whole row, including each other's lines."},{"oldText":"If it does, that review is a prerequisite for M1 and may have the longest lead time of all. Ask during M0.","newText":"If it does, its sign-off is a prerequisite for M1 going live and may have the longest lead time of all. Ask during M0, before any M1 code."},{"oldText":"Intake failures and failed mail sends show as a banner.","newText":"Intake failures and failed mail sends show as a banner to Leads and Triage. The banner holds only entries that still need an action, and Triage can dismiss any entry from the Health page."},{"oldText":"A message marked as automatic (an out-of-office reply, a bounce, a read receipt) is never posted, never answers an ask and never triggers a receipt or a notice.","newText":"A message marked as automatic (an out-of-office reply, a bounce, a read receipt) is never posted, never answers an ask and never triggers a receipt or a notice. A bounce that can be matched to something the app sent marks that send as failed, so a bounced ask shows \"Ask failed\" and a bounced receipt raises \"receipt not delivered\". An out-of-office reply to an open ask shows \"automatic reply received\" on that row."},{"oldText":"Before any code: sit with your boss for 15 minutes with the wireframe and three real firedrill emails from his inbox.","newText":"Before any code: sit with your boss for about an hour with three real firedrill emails from his inbox. Start with open questions before you show the wireframe: what he wants, how he tracks firedrills today, and whether he would rather have a shared list this week or an app in six to eight weeks. Write down three numbers as the baseline: how many firedrills are open today, how many status requests he sent in the last two weeks (his Sent Items will show), and how many firedrills ran late without his knowing. Before the visit, write down which answers would stop the app, so that the gate can fail."},{"oldText":"The same day, show the update screen to one manager and write down the first thing they say.","newText":"The same day, show the update screen to two or three managers and write down the first thing each one says."},{"oldText":"M1 work does not start until the Assignment is done. If the boss does not confirm that he wants one place for firedrill status that his managers update, work stops and the approach is revisited before anything is built.","newText":"M1 work does not start until the Assignment is done. - **Amendments accepted by the /autoplan CEO pass on 2026-11-11.** Each one is written into the plan body in the section it names. This list is the index, with the check that proves each one. Six are marked provisional: the author confirms or reverses them at the final gate.
  - The Assignment as a test that can fail (The Assignment, Success Criteria): open questions come before the wireframe, including "a shared list this week, or an app in six to eight weeks?". Three baseline numbers are written down (firedrills open today, status requests he sent in the last two weeks, firedrills that ran late unseen). The answers that would stop the app are written down before the visit. Two or three managers are asked, not one. The week-four criterion is set against the baseline. Provisional. Check: the visit's notes hold the three numbers and the pre-written stop answers.
  - Three answers before M1 code (Milestones, Dependencies): written answers on where the code may live, whether copies of email may be stored and whether a security review is required come before M1 starts. A required review's sign-off is a go-live prerequisite. If copies may not be stored, the fallback design keeps only confirmed fields and their quotes. Provisional. Check: the three answers are in the plan before the first M1 commit.
  - Before filing requests (Dependencies): two hours checking what ServiceNow dashboards, Planner and Lists already give the boss, and two questions for him about a second Triage person and a named owner in the org. Check: the notes say what each existing tool cannot do.
  - Teams (Updates and asks): the M2 ask no longer says it can post to Teams. Teams posting is recorded in TODOS.md. Provisional. Check: the M2 dependencies name no Teams permission.
  - First Triage person (Data model, Distribution Plan): created by the migration Job from a deploy setting, only when no active Triage person exists, and its sign-in value is corrected when that setting changes. The no-access page shows the value that was presented. Check: a fresh database gets exactly one Triage row, a second run adds none, and changing the setting changes that row.
  - Refused sign-ins (Data model, State and operations): each is recorded with the presented value for 30 days and shown on the Health page. Check: a sign-in with no roster row appears there with its value.
  - Deactivation (Data model): a person who owns an active assignment, or who is the primary Lead, cannot be made inactive. Done and closed history stays with them. Check: the attempt is refused and lists what to reassign, and a person whose only assignments are done or closed can be deactivated.
  - Status roll-up (Data model): the worst status among assignments that have not ended, in the order blocked, at risk, new, on track. Done is left out unless all are done. Check: a table of mixed-owner cases, including an ended blocked owner and a mix of new and done.
  - Done assignments (Data model, Updates and asks): a done assignment is made active again only by a form update with another status. An email reply never reopens it. Check: a form update with another status reopens it, and a reply on a done assignment posts nothing.
  - Closed filter and Reopen (Screens, Data model, Milestones): a Closed filter with a Reopen action ships in M1, and the closed list leaves M4. Closing cancels open asks and closes open needs. Reopening clears `closed_at`, restarts each stale clock at the reopen date, leaves ended asks and needs ended, and ends the assignment of an owner who is no longer active. If that would leave no assignment, Reopen requires picking an active owner first. Provisional. Check: reopen a firedrill that was closed for a month, and no assignment is stale that day or asked the next morning.
  - Board counts (Screens): open, due this week and overdue count firedrills. Stale, asked with no reply and waiting count assignments. Drafts to confirm counts intake items. Check: a firedrill with one stale and one waiting owner adds one to each of those two counts.
  - Banner (Screens): shown to Leads and Triage, it holds only entries that still need an action, and Triage can dismiss an entry from the Health page. Check: an expired digest leaves the banner once dismissed, and a manager never sees the banner.
  - Drafts (Data model): a draft is the intake item. Working values and picked owners live in `draft_fields`. The states gain `attached` and `failed`. Check: save draft, reload and publish keeps the edits, and a failed extraction shows in the banner and can be retried.
  - Ask record (Data model, Updates and asks): the asker field is `asked_by`. An update answers every unanswered, uncancelled ask on its assignment in one transaction, whatever the state of its send. An ask is cancelled when its assignment ends, when its firedrill closes or when a Lead or Triage cancels it. An ask whose send failed or expired is unsent. Cancelled and unsent asks are left out of the 70% figure. A wait or update entered for an owner carries `on_behalf_of`. Check: two asks, one sent and one failed, are both answered by one update, and a cancelled ask is on neither side of the figure.
  - Automatic asks (Updates and asks): one rule for a 7:00 run, "stale now and not yet asked in this stale period", keyed on `clock_start`. An unsent automatic ask gets a new send at the next 7:00 run while its assignment is still active, stale and not waiting in the same period, both switches are on and it has no open ask, and never a second ask. An unsent ask that a person clicked is not retried. Request update is always available on an active assignment and always creates a new ask, even while another is open. Check: a missed run is made up on the next run, a run executed twice creates one ask, and a failed send is retried on the same ask.
  - Switching automatic asks on (Updates and asks): turning them on, globally or for one firedrill, sends nothing for assignments already stale at that moment. They stay on the board as stale. The same holds for assignments made stale by lowering N. Provisional. Check: switch on with three stale assignments, run 7:00, zero asks.
  - Sending (Data model, Updates and asks): `mail_send` gains what the mail is about, a notice reason, the last attempt time, an expiry, and the states cancelled and expired. Mail text is built at send time. The sender rechecks before each attempt that an ask is still unanswered and not cancelled and, for an automatic ask, that its assignment is still stale and not waiting. Every kind of mail expires: after 24 hours, or at the end of its Monday for a digest. The Health page offers a retry only for mail that still applies. Check: an update posted between the 7:00 run and the send cancels that ask's mail, and mail left pending past its expiry is not sent.
  - Receipt not delivered (Updates and asks): an email-reply update whose receipt failed or expired is flagged on the board and on the Health page. Check: fail the receipt and the flag appears on that update.
  - Missed 7:00 run (Updates and asks, State and operations): if no successful scheduler run is recorded by 7:30 on a working day, the five-minute job does the work once and records it as that day's scheduler run. A digest is unique per Monday. Check: fail the 7:00 run and the asks and the digest go out that day exactly once, and with a healthy 7:00 run the five-minute job creates nothing.
  - Inbound mail (Data model, Intake, Stored email): every handled message gets an `inbound_message` row before it is moved, and leaves the Inbox for Processed, Not accepted or Automatic. The purge applies the retention periods to the mailbox copies. Check: a refused reply draws one notice, not one per run, and a 31-day-old Not accepted message is gone from the mailbox.
  - Bounces and out-of-office (Intake): a bounce that can be matched to something the app sent marks that send as failed, and an out-of-office reply to an open ask shows "automatic reply received" on the row. Check: a bounced ask shows "Ask failed", and a bounced receipt raises "receipt not delivered".
  - App mail other than asks (Intake): digests, receipts, notices and alerts carry no ask reference, set Reply-To to the app's mailbox, carry a marker in the subject and say that replies to them are not read. A message with that marker is filed as `other`, ignored and moved to "Automatic", whoever sent it. Check: a Lead's reply to the digest creates no draft, and a reply to a receipt posts nothing and draws no notice.
  - Reply-to-update off (Updates and asks, Intake, Data model): it has a switch. While it is off or was never shipped, new app-sent asks set Reply-To to the primary Lead and say replies are not tracked, and a reply to an older ask is filed as not accepted, with a notice pointing to its update link when the sender is an active roster member. Check: with the switch off, a reply to a new ask lands in that Lead's inbox, and a reply to an older ask posts nothing.
  - Status words in replies (Updates and asks): one trailing period, exclamation mark, comma or colon is ignored, and a first line ending in a question mark never matches. Check: "Done." sets done and "Done?" does not.
  - Time zone (Data model, State and operations): deploy-time configuration read by the app and every job, not an in-app setting. Check: day boundaries and the 7:00 run use the same zone in a test that sets it.
  - Operations records (Data model, State and operations, Distribution Plan): `job_run` backs the Health page and the alerts. The M1 Health page holds the purge, refused sign-ins and the Friday figures, and M2 adds mail. The maintainer's alert address is a setting. An alert fires on a change from working to failing and on recovery, not on every run, and sending is judged as a whole by its latest attempt. The Monday digest has its own switch. The 6:00 and 7:00 times rely on the cluster's CronJob time-zone field, and without it each job runs hourly and the app's zone decides when the work is due. The alerts depend on the cluster's alerting (Open Question 19). Without it, purge and scheduler alerts go by email once the app can send, and alerts about sending or about the five-minute job show only on the Health page and the banner. The manifests gain the purge CronJob and alert rules. Check: three failed runs in a row raise one alert, and a failed send raises none by email.
  - Friday figures (Success Criteria, Data model): computed at the daily job's 6:00 run on Fridays, stored in `weekly_figure` and shown on the Health page. Freshness is a snapshot at that run. The ask figures cover asks whose one-working-day window closed in the seven days before the run, on assignments that were active when asked, leaving out cancelled and unsent asks. "Within one working day" runs to the end of the next working day. A correction is a form update by the same owner within one working day of their email reply. If the Friday run fails, the next successful run computes that week's figures and marks them late. Check: fixed data gives the expected figures, and an ask sent on a Friday and answered on Monday counts as on time in the following week's figure.
  - Order inside M0 (Milestones, Dependencies): the Assignment comes first and the requests are filed after it, the same week. Provisional. Check: no request is filed before Demand Evidence holds the boss's answer.
  - Fallback clock and Approach A (Milestones, Approaches Considered): a required security review counts toward the three weeks, and Approach A is described as one list and two named flows. Check: the M1 row lists the review, and the fallback can be built from its description.
  - Open Question 2 outcome (Open Questions, The Assignment): the Assignment asks whether managers may see each other's firedrills. If the answer is no, a manager's board shows only their own, "Needs you" is for Leads and Triage only, co-owners of one firedrill still see its whole row, and the M2 ask says a reply is visible to the boss and to that firedrill's other owners. Check: the answer is recorded before M1 starts.
  - Open Question 13 (Milestones, Dependencies, The Assignment): the org's answer on storing copies of email is one of the three answers needed before M1 code and counts toward the three weeks. No real email is pasted in, and no copy of the Assignment's three emails is taken, before it. M1 goes live on the day the first real firedrill is published, and the four-week figures count from that day. An answer that forbids keeping copies switches the plan to the fallback design that keeps only confirmed fields and their quotes. Check: the answer is written in the plan before the first real paste.
  - The Assignment visit (The Assignment): about an hour with the boss, not 15 minutes, plus the conversations with two or three managers. Check: the visit's notes cover every question the plan lists.
  - Open Question 15 outcomes: a flat no to automatic asks means they are not built in M2 and go to TODOS.md, and "not yet" means built and off. If the boss declines the needs list for managers, the manager who raised a need still sees whether it was handled and can withdraw it. If he declines "Seen by", it is not built. Check: all three answers are recorded before M1 starts.
  - Open Question 17 outcome: a "link, not reply" answer means every manager is asked before the reply-to-update work starts, and a majority for the link returns the ORDER decision to the author. Check: the answers are written under Demand Evidence.
  - Tenant proofs (Open Question 16, Milestones): seven proofs are owed before the M2 build, each with a stated consequence if it fails. Check: each has a recorded result on the tenant.
  - What he tried before (The Assignment): if the boss already tried a shared list and dropped it, the reason is written down, and Approach A stops being the fallback unless that reason no longer applies. Check: Status Quo holds his answer.
  - New owners (Data model, Updates and asks): the app does not notify a new owner. The publish, add-owner and reassign steps say so, and from M2 the first app-sent ask on an assignment says who assigned it and when. Check: the three steps show the reminder.
  - Effort (Milestones): M0 about a day. M1 human ~3.5 weeks / CC ~2.5 days. M2 human ~4 weeks / CC ~2.5 days. All estimated.
  - Roles (Login and roles): the app authorizes on `person.role`, which group claims write at each sign-in when OIDC supplies them. The Lead, Manager and Triage entries list every action named elsewhere in the plan, including retrying or rejecting a failed intake item, an email's sharing switch, clearing a wait, and the Health page's retry and dismiss actions. Check: each action has a role test, and a changed group claim changes the stored role at the next sign-in.
  - API (API): the JSON form is a second representation of the listed routes with no caller of its own today. It stays at the author's request. Whether it is built in M1 is left to the engineering review and then the author, and until that is decided the M1 scope and estimate include it.
<!-- /autoplan-accepted:ceo -->

### Phase 2: Design review

Run by /autoplan on 2026-11-11 in a second session. The first session closed Phase 1 and stopped inside this phase before any design result was saved to the plan. This session re-verified the Phase 1 close against the unchanged plan text (implementation hash 0b028c91, the same text the CEO reviewer read) and ran this phase from the start. A draft list of design amendments left by the first session was reused where this session's reviewer supported it.

#### System audit and Step 0

- **UI scope.** Three described screens (Board, Confirm a draft, Manager update), and at least seven more that the plan only mentions: Firedrill detail, New firedrill, drafts queue, Closed list, roster, settings, Health, and the no-access page. Four kinds of email are also interface.
- **0A. Initial rating: 5/10.** The rules for stale, waiting, asks and mail are specified more tightly than the screens that must show them. The board is one long paragraph. No hierarchy is stated above the rows, most Lead actions have no home, the update screen has no after-Send state, and phone width appears only in the CEO review's notes. A 10 for this plan names what each role sees first, places every action from the role list on a screen, gives every screen its loading, empty, error, success and partial states, sets the words and badges once, and has pictures that match.
- **0B. DESIGN.md: none.** Universal principles are used. /design-consultation is recommended before the M1 interface is built (task T7).
- **0C. Existing leverage.** No code exists. The wireframe of 2026-11-10 is the only design artifact. It was read for this review. It is a calm table with text badges and no cards beyond five count tiles, which is a sound base. It contradicts the plan in at least eight places: one owner per row, five counts where the plan has seven plus Closed, no "Needs you", no overdue or waiting, no four-link ask, "your boss" on the form, a fold ("3 more open firedrills") that the stale-first sort makes unnecessary, and no phone frame.
- **0D. Focus areas.** All seven passes (pipeline override). No question asked.
- **Step 0.5, mockups: not generated.** The designer binary is present. Generating would send the plan's screen descriptions to an outside image service that the author has not approved for this work project, and the author declined a similar send in office hours (D10). The review is text-only against the wireframe. The author can run /design-shotgun later if that changes.

#### Voices

- **Outside (Codex): unavailable.** Codex is not installed. No outside model reviewed the design.
- **CLAUDE SUBAGENT (design, independent review): completed.** Input hash matched the phase snapshot (design 0b028c91). It read all 1926 lines and returned 28 findings: 4 critical, 13 high, 11 medium. It did not open the wireframe.

| # | Sev | Finding | Disposition |
|---|---|---|---|
| F1 | Critical | No firedrill detail view, so most Lead actions have no home | Applied: Screen 4, Firedrill, with an action-location check |
| F2 | Critical | Row anatomy undefined, no precedence among state labels | Applied: two-level row, clamp rule, one ask state per line with precedence |
| F3 | High | No stacking order or bound above the rows | Applied: banner, needs capped at three, chips, rows |
| F4 | High | Seven counts mix three units and double as filters | Applied: units named, result line, drafts moved to a link, single-select |
| F5 | High | Nothing surfaces blocked or at-risk work | Applied provisionally: status weight in the sort, two chips (gate item) |
| F6 | High | Managers get the boss's hierarchy | Applied provisionally: "My assignments" landing, list title by role (gate item) |
| F7 | Medium | Leads see a banner they cannot act on | Applied provisionally: a Lead's banner is narrowed (gate item) |
| F8 | Critical | Update screen is missing most of its states | Applied: order, required fields, after-Send, six edge states, double Send |
| F9 | High | M1 ask records "Asked" before anything is sent | Applied: "Sent it?" step, copy fallback. "Ask all stale owners" is provisional (gate item) |
| F10 | High | Sign-in and access states on the ask link | Applied: no-access copy, network line in the ask, M0 reachability note |
| F11 | Medium | Board loading, error and empty states | Applied: outage page, empty copy by role, inline action errors |
| F12 | High | Confirm screen gaps | Applied: quotes under fields, candidate picker, required fields, landing, narrow layout |
| F13 | Medium | Irreversible actions have no confirm or undo | Applied: confirmations with numbers, undo on Handled |
| F14 | High | Seven or more screens exist only as mentions | Applied: one line each, built from the same parts |
| F15 | High | The M1 loop probably breaks at the reply | Applied: "Paste reply" on the owner line, posted-for share reported |
| F16 | High | The boss gets no signal that a forward landed | Applied in part: "New" mark, and the publish email copies him. A triage turnaround promise is not added |
| F17 | High | The needs loop does not close for the manager | Applied in part: Handled note and a "tell them" email. A notice by app mail in M2 goes to TODOS.md |
| F18 | Medium | "Seen by" over-claims | Applied in part: marked only when rendered under the filter in use. The label stays (author's D12) |
| F19 | Medium | An email reply updates text but usually not status | Applied: "status unchanged" mark. A Lead already sets status by posting for the owner |
| F20 | Medium | M2 asks arrive as several emails in one morning | Applied: subject and first line written, short link shape stated |
| F21 | High | The gate visit shows the boss a stale wireframe | Applied: redraw before the Assignment |
| F22 | Medium | No visual vocabulary | Applied: badge inventory in three emphasis levels. Typeface and color wait for a DESIGN.md |
| F23 | Medium | UI labels are inconsistent | Applied: one term per thing, Lead named, blocked versus waiting helper. "Stale" stays |
| F24 | Medium | Time units mixed and unlabelled | Applied: working days on stale, calendar days on overdue and need age |
| F25 | High | Phone width and accessibility absent from the body | Applied: 360-pixel update screen, stacked board, keyboard and focus rules |
| F26 | Medium | Digest, receipt and notice content not designed | Applied: digest order and subject, all-clear text, one line per notice reason |
| F27 | Medium | A waiting line may show its reason twice | Applied: the wait replaces the last update on that line |
| F28 | Medium | Open Question 2 forks three screens, one fork designed | Applied: counts and tabs under the restricted answer |

The Claude column is this review's reading of the plan and wireframe, using the subagent's evidence: check 2 from F3 (three blocks compete above the rows), check 4 from F4 (the counts strip filters, counts and links to a queue), check 5 from the wireframe's five count tiles, which the amendments replace with chips. The subagent was not asked the litmus questions. With no outside voice, no cell is confirmed.

#### Pass 1. Information architecture: 4/10, now 8/10

Classifier: OPERATE (an app surface where people finish a task). The body stated a sort order for rows and nothing else about order. Fixed by the amendments: the order above the rows, the two-level row, Screen 4, the manager landing, and a home for every action.

```
BOARD (Lead)                              MY ASSIGNMENTS (manager landing)
+--------------------------------------+  +------------------------------+
| banner: one line, only when it applies|  | Waiting on <Lead>: my needs  |
| Needs you (max 3, oldest first) +N    |  | my owner lines, asks flagged |
| chips: Open | owners stale | asked,   |  |   [Update] inline            |
|   no reply | waiting | due this week |  | tab: Whole board             |
|   | overdue | blocked or at risk |    |  +------------------------------+
|   ready to close     Closed  Drafts   |
| result line: "4 stale owners in 1.."  |  FIREDRILL (screen 4)
| ROW  firedrill line (title, From, due)|  header fields (Triage edits)
|      owner line: status, update, ask  |  one block per owner + actions
|      owner line ...                   |  updates, newest first
| ROW ...  (stale first, then status,   |  emails + sharing switch
|           then soonest due)           |  Close / Add owner / auto-asks
+--------------------------------------+
```

If only three things fit: what needs him, what is stale, and the ask button. Remaining gap to 10: the pictures. The wireframe is redrawn before the Assignment (T1).

#### Pass 2. Interaction states: 3/10, now 8/10

| Feature | Loading | Empty | Error | Success | Partial |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Board | server-rendered page, no skeleton | by role: "Forward the email to <Triage>", "Nothing is assigned to you", "Paste an email". Each filter has its own line | outage page when the database or sign-in is down. Inline error in the cell for a failed action | rows, "New" mark on rows since the last visit | "+N other owners" under an assignment filter |
| Needs you | with the page | Lead: "Nothing is waiting on you". Manager: hidden | inline error on Handled | Handled with note, undo for a few seconds | "N more" past three |
| Ask cell | second click ignored while the first is in flight | "Not yet asked" | "Ask failed" with "Ask again" | "Asked just now. Sent it?" then "Asked 2h ago, no reply" | waiting: "Waiting until <date>" with "Ask anyway" |
| Confirm a draft | with the page | failed extraction opens an empty draft | someone else published first: told and shown the result | lands on the new firedrill, "Tell the owner" | unfilled date candidates listed, several matches listed |
| Manager update | with the page | first visit line | six edge states, each with one message | confirmation quoting the post | text typed before a session expired survives sign-in |
| No access | n/a | n/a | shows the presented value and the Triage contact | n/a | n/a |
| Other screens | with the page | one empty line each | failed save shown inline | saved | n/a |

Remaining gap to 10: the Health, roster and settings screens have one line each, not a state row each. That is enough for a Triage-only surface at this stage.

#### Pass 3. Journey and emotional arc: 5/10, now 8/10

| Step | User does | User feels | Plan specifies? |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Boss forwards an email to the author (M1) | "Did that land?" | Now: publish email copies him, row carries "New" |
| 2 | Boss opens the board | Wants the answer in five seconds | Now: needs, chips, stale-first rows in a fixed order |
| 3 | Boss clicks Request update | Unsure the mail went | Now: "Sent it?" with Yes and Cancel |
| 4 | Manager gets the ask on a phone | Mild annoyance, suspicion of four raw links | Now: short links, link text says it opens a short form, network line |
| 5 | Manager hits Reply instead (M1) | Believes they answered | Now: the ask says a reply reaches the Lead, and "Paste reply" makes posting it ten seconds |
| 6 | Manager opens the form | Wants out fast | Now: status, one box, Send. Extras folded |
| 7 | Manager presses Send | "Did it work, who sees it?" | Now: confirmation with the quote, the audience and "Seen by appears here" |
| 8 | Manager raised a need | Waits to be heard | Now: Handled note and "tell them" email |
| 9 | Boss, week four | Trust or abandonment | Stale shows working days, waiting and overdue shown apart, digest mirrors the board |

Five seconds: he sees what needs him. Five minutes: he asks and it is answered. Five years: the board is where firedrill status lives. Remaining gap to 10: the manager still learns a need was handled only by email the Lead chooses to send, or by looking (a notice by app mail is deferred).

#### Pass 4. Generic-UI risk: 6/10, now 8/10

- **Hard rejections: none.** The surface is a table with layout, not stacked cards.
- **Litmus:** as in the scorecard. After the amendments, check 2 is yes (the stale-first rows under a bounded needs list), check 4 is yes (chips filter, links navigate) and check 5 is yes (no count tiles).
- **What is specific to this product, and now dominates:** staleness in working days as the main signal, quotes as evidence under each drafted field, and "Needs you". Copy is utility language throughout.
- **App rules:** calm surface, dense but readable, minimal chrome. Met in the wireframe and kept.
- Remaining gap to 10: typeface, color and themed browser surfaces are not chosen. That belongs to a DESIGN.md.

#### Pass 5. Design system: 2/10, now 5/10

No DESIGN.md exists, so this pass rates what the plan itself specifies. Before: nothing. Now: a badge inventory in three emphasis levels, no color-only state, body text at least 16 pixels with 4.5 to 1 contrast, touch targets of 44 pixels, plain-text mail. Still unspecified: typeface, color variables, spacing scale and component names. This stays open and is the reason the overall score stays below 8. Recommended: /design-consultation before the M1 interface is built (T7).

#### Pass 6. Responsive and accessibility: 2/10, now 8/10

Before: absent from the plan body. Now: the update screen is designed first at 360 pixels, the board stacks one block per firedrill below about 720 pixels, Confirm puts the email above the fields at that width, every action is reachable by keyboard in reading order, focus follows a partial page swap, and the banner is announced when it changes. Remaining gap to 10: landmarks and a tablet layout are not stated.

#### Pass 7. Unresolved design decisions

| Decision | If deferred | State |
|---|---|---|
| Sort by status after stale, and a "Blocked or at risk" chip | A fresh blocked row hides among on-track rows | Provisional, gate item d7 |
| Managers land on "My assignments" | Managers hunt for their own lines on the boss's board | Provisional, gate item d8 |
| "Ask all stale owners" in M1 | Four owners take four compose windows | Provisional, gate item d9 |
| A Lead's banner holds only what he can act on | Purge failures sit above his board | Provisional, gate item d10 |
| "Tell the owner" email on by default at publish, add and reassign | A new owner learns of a firedrill only when first asked | Provisional, gate item d11 |
| Typeface, color and spacing | Browser defaults ship | Open, needs /design-consultation |
| Whether the app's route works from a phone | The manager's first visit is a timeout | Open until M0 answers it |

Resolved in this pass by the amendments: 23. Provisional for the gate: 5. Open: 2.
