- **Design amendments accepted by the /autoplan design pass on 2026-11-11.** They specify the interface that the rules above need. Where this list differs from the three screen descriptions under "Screens", this list governs. Five are marked provisional: the author confirms or reverses them at the final gate.
  - Screen 4, Firedrill: the row title links to it. It holds the header fields, then one block per owner (status, last update, ask state, that owner's actions), then updates newest first as a plain list that includes the kept updates of ended assignments, then the emails with each one's sharing switch (Lead and Triage only), then Close, Add owner and the automatic-asks switch at the foot. An owner block carries Remove, Reassign, Set or clear waiting and "Post for <owner>". Triage edits fields in the header. Only Request update, Cancel ask, Update (own line), "Paste reply" and Handled stay on the board row. Until M4 this is the only place to read an update older than the latest. Check: a table in the engineering plan maps every action in the role list to the board row, this screen or both, and each is reachable.
  - Board row: a firedrill line (title, one-line description, "From", due date or overdue badge, ticket links) over one line per owner (owner, status, last update and its age, stale or waiting badge, ask cell). The roll-up status is used for sorting, filtering and the digest, and is not shown beside the per-owner statuses. Owner lines are ordered stale, blocked, at risk, new, on track, waiting, done. Under a filter that counts assignments, the row shows the matching lines and "+N other owners". There is no fold: every open row is rendered. Update text is clamped to three lines with an inline expand. A waiting line whose latest update is the record of that wait shows "Waiting until <date>: <reason>" once, in place of the last update. Check: a four-owner firedrill with mixed statuses renders one firedrill line and four owner lines in that order, and a waiting line shows its reason once.
  - Above the rows, in this order: the banner as one line with a count, "Needs you" capped at three, oldest first, with "N more", one row of chips, then the rows. Each chip names its unit ("4 owners stale"), and a result line above the rows says what the filter found ("4 stale owners in 1 firedrill"). Closed is a text link, not a chip. "Drafts to confirm" is a link to the drafts queue for Leads and Triage only. A "Ready to close" chip lists firedrills whose owners are all done, with Close on the row. Chips are single-select, the default is Open, a chip at zero is disabled, the chosen chip is visibly marked with a clear control, and the chosen filter and row are in the page address so the digest can link to them. A row published since the viewer's last board view carries a "New" mark until that view ends. Check: at 1366 by 768 with a banner and three needs, two rows are visible.
  - Sort gives status a weight: stale rows first, then rows that are not stale by blocked, at risk, new, on track, then soonest due. A "Blocked or at risk" chip counts firedrills by roll-up. Provisional. Check: a fresh blocked row due in three weeks sits above a fresh on-track row due next week.
  - Managers land on "My assignments": their active owner lines with Update inline and any open ask flagged, and the full board on a second tab. For anyone who is not a Lead, the needs list is titled "Waiting on <Lead name>". This page is where notices and the single-link fallback point. Provisional. Check: a manager with two assignments sees both before any other row.
  - "Ask all stale owners" in M1: one button opens a single prefilled email addressed to every owner with a stale assignment, with one link to "My assignments", and records one ask per assignment. The four-link ask per row stays. Provisional. Check: five stale assignments produce one compose window and five recorded asks.
  - Ask cell: one state per owner line, each with its text and action. No ask ("Request update"). M1 clicked ("Asked just now. Sent it?" with Yes, Cancel and "Copy the ask text" for a device with no mail program). Asked with no reply ("Asked 2h ago, no reply" with "Ask again"). Auto-asked. Auto-asked with no reply. Ask failed (with "Ask again"). Automatic reply received. Answered (the cell returns to "Request update"). While waiting, the cell reads "Waiting until <date>" with "Ask anyway". A done line has no ask control. A new assignment with no ask or update reads "Not yet asked" with the button emphasized. When several apply, the cell shows the first of: Ask failed, Automatic reply received, the newest open ask, waiting. An action that fails shows an inline error in the cell and changes nothing, and a second click while the first is in flight records nothing more. Check: each state renders from fixture data, and a line with a failed ask and an older open ask shows "Ask failed".
  - Update screen order: a context line (firedrill, due date, who is asking), the status choice, "what changed", Send. Below a divider sit two collapsed sections: "Need something from <Lead name>?" and "Waiting on a date?". "Seen by" and "need handled" sit inside the quote of the last update. A status is required, and none is preselected when the screen is opened from the board. Text is optional, as it is for an email reply. One Send makes one update, pressing Send twice still makes one, and a wait's reason is stored on the assignment and shown beside the text. One helper line under the status choice says what blocked means (stuck, someone must act) and what waiting means (nothing can move before a known date). Under each field a helper line names who will read it, worded to follow the answers to Open Questions 2 and 15. A first visit shows one line saying what the board is and who reads it. Check: the form posts with a status and no text, a double Send posts one update, and the helper text changes with the visibility setting.
  - Arriving from a status link: the heading says "Not sent yet", the button carries the status ("Send: On track"), and the email's link text says "On track (opens a short form)". Opening the link changes nothing by itself. A link from an older ask opens with its status chosen and a line saying when that ask was sent. The link is a short path on the app's own address with no long token, so four plain links in a mailto body read as what they are. Check: fetching the link without pressing Send posts no update.
  - After Send: a confirmation quotes what was posted, says who can see it, says which asks it answered, says "Seen by appears here once <Lead name> has viewed it", lists the manager's other assignments that need an update, and offers "post a correction". Updates are never edited, only superseded, and Triage can remove one. Check: the confirmation shows the posted text and the remaining assignments.
  - Update screen edge states, one message each: not the owner (says who owns it), assignment ended (says who has it now), assignment done (says that sending another status reopens it), firedrill closed, ask already answered (shows the answer and still allows a new update), and a session that expired with text typed (the text survives sign-in). Check: one test per state.
  - Sign-in and access: the no-access page shows the value that was presented, says in one sentence that the roster does not hold it yet, and names the Triage person to contact. The ask email says in one line where the link works (for example "on the company network or VPN"), worded from what the M0 requests learn about the route. Check: the no-access page names the contact, and the M0 notes say whether the route is reachable from a phone.
  - Confirm a draft: each drafted field shows its supporting quote under it, and clicking the quote highlights it in the email. Date candidates that were not filled are listed under the due date field, each with its quote and a "use this" action. A match panel at the top of the right column shows the matched firedrill's title, due date and owners. Choosing Attach replaces the fields with a diff of what would change ("Due: Nov 21 to Dec 5, apply?") and the sharing switch. With more than one match the panel lists them and the person picks one. The sharing switch sits under the email and names who will see it when on. Title and short description are required to publish, and the description is typed by hand in M1 with a length limit and a counter. A failed extraction opens an empty draft to fill by hand. An identical paste opens the earlier item when it still exists, and otherwise says it was rejected before and offers to draft it anyway. "Not a firedrill" asks for confirmation because it deletes the email. After Publish the person lands on the new firedrill. If someone else published the same draft first, the second person is told and shown the result. A draft shows how many days remain before it expires. Below about 720 pixels the email sits above the fields, folded to its newest message. Check: attach with a changed due date shows the diff, and a failed extraction can still be published by hand.
  - "Paste reply": on an owner line, a Lead or Triage opens one text box with an optional status and posts it for that owner in one step. This is the expected M1 path when a manager answers the ask by replying, and the plan says so in the M1 ask's own text ("a reply reaches <Lead name>, not the board"). The Friday figures also report the share of updates that were posted for an owner. Check: paste and post takes one screen and marks the update "posted for".
  - "Needs you" states: for a Lead with none, "Nothing is waiting on you". For a manager with none, the list is hidden. Handled takes an optional one-line note that the manager who raised the need sees with who handled it and when, and it offers a prefilled "tell them" email. Handled can be undone for a few seconds. A need closed because its firedrill closed is labelled "closed with the firedrill", never "handled". Need ages show calendar days. Check: each label renders from fixture data, and a note typed at Handled shows on the manager's update screen.
  - Banner and outages: a Lead's banner says what it means for him ("2 forwarded emails are not on the board yet. <Triage name> has been alerted"), and Triage's adds a link to the Health page. When the database or the identity provider is down the app shows a plain outage page, never an empty board. Check: with the database stopped, the board request returns the outage page.
  - A Lead's banner holds only entries that change what he sees or can act on: delayed intake and asks that failed. Job, purge and digest failures show to Triage only. This narrows the earlier rule that every entry shows to Leads and Triage. Provisional. Check: a failed purge shows on Triage's banner and not on a Lead's.
  - First run and empty states: Triage gets a setup checklist (roster, primary Lead, ServiceNow address), and nothing can be published until it is complete. Empty copy by role and milestone: for a Lead in M1, "Forward the email to <Triage name>". For a manager, "Nothing is assigned to you". For Triage, "Paste an email". Each filter has its own positive empty line, such as "Nothing is stale", which differs from the first-run line. At launch, Triage asks each owner of an already open firedrill for a first update, so day one is not a wall of "New". Check: a fresh deploy shows the checklist, and each role sees its own empty line.
  - Actions with side effects confirm with numbers: Close ("cancels 2 open asks, closes 1 need"), Remove owner, Reassign and Reopen. Check: the confirmation's numbers match the data.
  - New owners: Publish, Add owner and Reassign each end on "Tell the owner", a prefilled email that is on by default, names the firedrill and links to it. At Publish it copies the Lead who forwarded the email, so he knows it is on the board. This replaces the earlier wording that those steps only say the app does not notify. Provisional. Check: publishing with two owners offers one prefilled email to both.
  - "Seen by": nothing is shown until an update has been seen. An update is marked seen only when its row or its Firedrill screen was rendered for a Lead under the filter then in use. Check: an update on a row hidden by the current filter is not marked seen.
  - An update that arrived by email reply and named no status shows "status unchanged" beside "via email", so a reader does not take the old status as confirmed. Check: a reply with no status word renders both marks.
  - Emails: the digest lists, in the board's order, open needs, overdue and due this week, stale, then new, with the counts in its subject and each list capped with "N more". Its all-clear reads "Nothing needs you this week: no open needs, nothing overdue or stale, nothing new". The M2 ask's subject is the firedrill title and due date, so several asks on one morning differ. Its first line is the question ("Situation Room is asking for <Lead name>: no update in 4 working days on <title>"), then the four links, then the reply instruction with the visibility note beside it. Each notice reason has its own one-line wording. All app mail is plain text with full links. Check: each template renders from fixture data, and two asks on one morning have different subjects.
  - Badges: one inventory covers the five statuses, stale, overdue, waiting, the ask states, "via email", "posted for", "status unchanged", "New" and "receipt not delivered", in three levels of emphasis (needs action, changed, informational). The status `new` is shown as "No update yet". No badge relies on color alone: each has text, and the top level also has an outline. Stale shows working days ("STALE 6 working days") and overdue shows calendar days. Dates show the year when it is not the current one. Times are relative on the page for seven days and absolute after that and on hover. Check: one page renders every badge.
  - Phone width and access: the update screen is designed first at 360 pixels wide, with touch targets of at least 44 pixels. Below about 720 pixels the board becomes one stacked block per firedrill. Body text is at least 16 pixels with a contrast ratio of at least 4.5 to 1. Every action is reachable by keyboard in reading order, focus moves to the changed element after a partial page swap, and the banner is announced to screen readers when it changes. Check: the update screen passes at 360 pixels, and a keyboard-only pass completes ask, update and Handled.
  - Words: one term per thing. The button is "Request update" and the thing it creates is an "ask". The form and the emails name the Lead ("Need something from <Lead name>?"), never "your boss". The row's "source" is the person or group that sent the email, shown as "From". The owner picker lists active managers and Leads. Check: a deployment with two Leads shows the right name on each ask.
  - Other screens, one line each, built from the same parts: "New firedrill" (subject and body fields, Paste and draft). Drafts queue (drafts oldest first with days left, failed items marked, for Leads and Triage). Closed list (newest first, Reopen). Roster (people with role, sign-in value, mailbox address, active, for Triage). Settings (the values the Data model lists, each with who changed it and when, for Triage). Health (as State and operations describes). Each has its empty line and shows a failed save inline. Check: each screen renders empty and with fixture data.
  - If the answer to Open Question 2 is no: a manager's chips and result line count only the firedrills they own, "My assignments" is their only tab, and the wireframe's restricted variant shows it. Check: a manager who owns one of five firedrills sees "1 open".
  - Wireframe: it is redrawn before the Assignment, because the old sketch contradicts the plan in at least eight places and the three things the boss is asked to agree to are not on it. Frames: the board at 1366 by 768 with needs, a banner and a four-owner row, the ask cell in every state, the manager landing, the Firedrill screen, Confirm with a match and with several date candidates, the update screen at 360 pixels in its arrive, sent, ended and done states, and the emails as text. Both the open and the restricted variants of Open Questions 2 and 15 are drawn. Check: the Assignment is held with the new frames.
<!-- /autoplan-accepted:design -->

### Phase 2.5: DX review

Run by /autoplan on 2026-11-11. Mode: DX POLISH (pipeline override). Product type: an internal API and service with deploy manifests, read from the plan's routes, jobs and Distribution Plan. No question was asked.

#### Step 0

```
TARGET DEVELOPER PERSONA
========================
Who:       The backup maintainer: an engineer in the org (or the author in six months,
           or an AI coding agent working for either) who clones the repo to run, fix or extend it.
Context:   The author is on leave and intake has stopped, or a change is needed.
Tolerance: About 15 minutes from clone to a board they can click on.
Expects:   A README, one command to run it, sample data, tests that pass.
```

Evidence for the persona: the README is one line, the plan names one maintainer and asks for a second Triage person, and its effort figures assume an AI coding agent does much of the build.

**Developer perspective (0B, predicted from the plan, nothing was run).** I clone the repo. The README says "situation-room" and nothing else. I open the design document: it is long, and its last two lists say they overrule what I just read. I look for how to start it. There is no compose file and no seed data. The app needs a sign-in provider and a roster row before it will show me a page, and the first roster row is made by a deploy Job that reads a setting whose name I cannot find. I guess at a database address. I cannot tell which settings exist. I give up on running it locally and ask for access to the cluster, which takes days. When a job fails later, the Health page says "failed" and I cannot tell a rejected relay from an expired credential.

**Benchmark (0C).** Clock: clean machine with a container runtime, from clone to a seeded board in a browser.

| Tool | Start to result | Time and evidence type | DX choice | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Full Stack FastAPI Template | clone, one setup step that writes `.env`, one command to start the stack | not quantified by the source (image build time dominates) | Compose is the golden path, secrets generated for you | github.com/fastapi/full-stack-fastapi-template, read through a search summary |
| "git clone and run" bar | clone to running | under 15 minutes (an author's bar, not a study) | one entry command, seed data, a documented reset | aaronstannard.com/git-clone-and-run |
| Situation Room as planned | no local path. Deployed path: 14 steps, 7 of them waiting on other people | days to weeks, counted from the plan by the reviewer | none stated | this plan |

Target: Competitive, under 5 minutes for the local run. The deployed path stays days to weeks and is governed by approvals, not by this target.

**Magical moment (0D).** One command, then a browser shows a board that already has a stale four-owner firedrill and an open need, and clicking Request update writes the ask to the mail sink. Delivery vehicle: seed fixture plus a development sign-in, the lowest-effort one that reaches the target.

**Journey (0F).**

| Stage | Developer does | Friction | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1. Discover | Opens the repo | One-line README | fixed: quickstart is an M1 deliverable |
| 2. Install | Clones, starts services | No compose file, no configuration reference | fixed: local run and configuration table |
| 3. Hello world | Opens the board | Sign-in needs a provider and a roster row | fixed provisionally: development sign-in (gate item d12) |
| 4. Real usage | Changes a rule, runs tests | No clock control or mail sink named outside the test notes | fixed: both are part of the local run |
| 5. Debug | Reads a failure | "failed" with no cause, code or runbook link | fixed: error contract and stored causes |
| 6. Upgrade | Deploys a change | Migration Job failure undefined, restore loses replies | fixed: Job behavior and restore runbook |

**First-time developer report (0G, predicted).**

```
T+0:00  Clone. README is one line.
T+1:00  Open the design doc. Find "Shape": Python, FastAPI, "if your team runs another stack, use that".
T+4:00  Look for a run command. None. Look for settings. Names are not listed.
T+8:00  Realize sign-in is required and the first user comes from a deploy Job.

#### Passes

**Pass 1. Getting started: 2/10, now 7/10.** Nothing local existed. Now a three-command local run with a stated five-minute target. Not 10: it is specified, not built or timed, and the deployed path still takes weeks.

**Pass 2. API design: 4/10, now 6/10.** Routes addressed the firedrill while the data belongs to an assignment, and nothing covered idempotency, errors or versioning. The contract is now stated for the case the JSON form is built. Not higher: whether it is built at all is undecided, and no agent-facing surface exists to assess.

**Pass 3. Errors: 4/10, now 7/10.**

```

Mail send fails              | dependency | part | pass| pass     | fail | fail    | fail  | fail      | part    | CAP6:context
Extraction failed            | unexpected | fail | pass| part     | fail | fail    | fail  | fail      | pass    | CAP6:trace
Sign-in refused, no row      | usage      | pass | pass| pass     | fail | pass    | N/A   | pass      | fail    | none

Before, Triage saw "failed". After, for a rejected relay: `MAIL_RELAY_REJECTED: ask for assignment 42 to m.t@example was refused by the relay (550). Nothing was sent. Fix: see runbook "relay rejects mail", then Retry on the Health page.` No CAP4 was confirmed, and the three places that would become one are now stated. Not higher: codes and wording exist only as a contract.

**Pass 4. Documentation: 3/10, now 6/10.** One-line README and a governing document with three layers. Now five documentation deliverables in M1, runbooks keyed by code, and a task to fold the plan into one governing text. Not higher until that fold is done.

**Pass 5. Upgrade path: 4/10, now 6/10.** Additive migrations and a rollback path were already in the CEO review. Added: migration Job failure behavior, permanent emailed links, the restore rehearsal. No versioning policy for the JSON form, which waits on whether it exists.

**Pass 6. Developer environment: 2/10, now 7/10.** Compose, seed, mail sink, settable clock and a test mailbox profile. CI is still Open Question 6.

**Pass 7. Community: 3/10, now 4/10.** An internal tool with one maintainer. What matters here is a second person who can run it, which the plan now asks the boss for and the local run makes possible. No issue found beyond that.

**Pass 8. Measurement: 3/10, now 6/10.** The clean-clone run by the second Triage person is timed once and written in the README. No telemetry is added for it, by choice: one timed run is the simplest measure of the target.

- **Developer experience amendments accepted by the /autoplan DX pass on 2026-11-11.** The developer here is whoever builds, runs or inherits the app: the author, the second Triage person, an AI coding agent, and later any caller of the JSON form. Two are marked provisional: the author confirms or reverses them at the final gate.
  - Local run (M1 deliverable): a fresh clone reaches a seeded board in three commands and under five minutes. It needs a compose file with Postgres, a seed command that loads a fixture board (a four-owner firedrill, stale, waiting, overdue, an open need, a draft to confirm), a mail sink that writes what would be sent to disk, a settable clock, and a development sign-in that lets the person pick a seeded Lead, Manager or Triage. The development sign-in works only when an explicit local flag is set, the app refuses to start when that flag is set together with any OIDC or proxy setting, and no manifest in the repo sets it. Provisional, because it adds M1 scope and a sign-in bypass that must never ship. Check: a person who has not seen the repo follows the README on a clean machine and is timed, and a start with the local flag plus an OIDC setting exits non-zero.
  - Configuration reference: one table lists every deploy-time key and every Secret name with required or optional, default, an example and which component reads it. The app and each job check their configuration at start, exit non-zero and name the missing or invalid key, never its value. Settings changed in the app are validated on save: the time zone is a known zone, the ServiceNow address is a URL, N is at least 1, the primary Lead is an active Lead. Every setting has a listed key, default, range and who may change it. Check: starting with one required key removed prints that key's name and exits non-zero.
  - The stack is decided, not proposed, before M1 code, together with Open Question 6, and written at the top of the README. Check: the README names it.
  - Proofs: each of the two M0 proofs and the seven tenant proofs has a written procedure that prints pass or fail and the observed value, as a script where one can be written, and its result is recorded in the plan. Check: nine recorded results.
  - A test target for mail (M2): before the real mailbox is used, mail features run against a test mailbox under a configuration profile that sends only to an allowlist of addresses. Check: with the profile on, a send to an address off the list is refused and recorded.
  - Error contract, for every failure the app, a job or the JSON form reports: a stable code, what failed with the actual values, what to do next, and a link to the runbook section for that code. No secret, token, connection string or email text appears in `job_run.detail`, alert bodies, the Health page or logs. Check: one test per code asserts the fields, and a test feeds a connection string through a failing job and finds none of it in the output.
  - Failures are signalled as failures: a request to the JSON form or to a partial page update without a valid session gets 401 with a problem body, never a redirect to sign-in or a 200 page. The outage page is served with 503 and Retry-After. Every job exits non-zero on failure and writes the cause to its error output. JSON errors use RFC 9457 problem bodies with the same codes. Check: an expired session on a partial update returns 401, the outage page returns 503, and a job that fails exits non-zero.
  - Stored causes: `mail_send` gains `last_error_code` and a redacted `last_error_detail`, shown on the Health page. A failed `intake_item` stores an error code and a log reference, never email text. A failed mailbox pull is classed as token expired, grant removed, egress blocked, throttled or other, each with its runbook section, and when the mailbox cannot be reached the Lead's banner reads "Mail intake has been down since <time>", because the count of waiting mail is unknown. `job_run.detail` is structured: code, cause, configuration key names and a log reference. Check: a relay rejection and an expired credential show different codes on the Health page.
  - Jobs that do not run: the Health page marks a job "overdue" when it has not finished within twice its interval. Each CronJob has a deadline and forbids overlapping runs, so a hung run cannot block later ones. Check: stop the five-minute job and the page shows it overdue.
  - Migration Job: it runs migrations in a transaction, exits non-zero naming the cause when the database is unreachable, a migration fails or the first-Triage setting is missing, and a failed Job stops the rollout with the previous version still serving. Check: a rollout with the setting missing does not replace the running app.
  - First Triage person, refined: a changed deploy setting corrects the seeded row only when the mailbox address is unchanged, which is the same person under a new sign-in value. A different mailbox address is a different person: the Job creates that person as Triage and leaves the earlier row and its history alone. Check: changing both values yields two rows.
  - Restore runbook: before a restore, switch off automatic asks, the digest and sending. After it, pull the "Processed" folder again from the backup's time so replies that had already left the Inbox are posted, expire the restored pending mail, then switch back on. Check: a rehearsal restores a backup taken before three replies and ends with those three updates on the board and no duplicate ask.
  - Notices: the fifth notice to one address in a day says that further replies today will not be answered and links to "My assignments". Check: the fifth notice carries that line.
  - "Ask all stale owners" puts each asked line in the same "Asked just now. Sent it?" state as a single ask. Check: Cancel on the prompt cancels all the asks it recorded.
  - A sign-in whose group claims map to no role gets its own page: it says which groups arrived, that the fix is in the directory and not the roster, and whom to contact, and it is recorded with refused sign-ins under its own reason. Check: the page lists the groups presented.
  - Names: one glossary of about ten terms maps the word on the screen to its table, route and job, and the code follows it. It settles ask and `update_request`, the five-minute job, the daily job (which purges and stores the Friday figures), the three meanings of `source`, and `state` versus `status`. Check: the glossary is in the repo before the first migration.
  - Emailed links are permanent: the address scheme for status links, "My assignments", a firedrill and a board filter is written down, and an address that has been sent keeps working or redirects. Check: a test holds one example of each address.
  - Fields the design amendments need: the person's last board view time (for the "New" mark), a dismissed time and who on each banner entry, a note on a handled need, removed time and who on an update that Triage removed, and both homes for a wait's reason (the assignment holds the current reason, and the update that recorded the wait keeps it as history). Undo on Handled clears the handled fields. The setup checklist is computed from the roster and settings and stores nothing. Check: each behavior in the design amendments names the field it reads.
  - Configuration, not constants: the ticket prefix list and the 6:00, 7:00 and 7:30 times are configuration. The retry count, mail expiry, notice cap, date window, status words, subject marker and sender name are constants in one module. Check: a ticket prefix added in configuration is linked on the board.
  - Non-working dates: a setting lists dates that are not working days, read by the one date function, so a holiday week does not turn every assignment stale or bend the Friday figure. Provisional, because the plan said the app does not know holidays. Check: with a date listed, an update on the day before is not stale one working day sooner.
  - Documentation that ships with M1: a README quickstart, the configuration reference, a deploy guide with the Secret names, one paragraph stating the rule that everything is an event on an assignment, and the M0 request texts with the exact Graph permission names. Runbooks added to the four already planned: rotating each secret, a job that is hung or overdue, egress to Graph blocked, rolling back after a schema change, and switching each mail feature off in an incident. Each runbook opens with the codes that lead to it and holds commands that can be pasted. Check: every error code links to a runbook section that exists.
  - One governing document: before M1 code, both amendment lists are folded into the body, the review history moves to its own file, and one copy in the repo governs. Check: the plan has no "this list governs" sentence left.
  - The JSON form, if it is built: updates and asks are addressed to the assignment in the path, the generated OpenAPI document is published, a POST accepts an idempotency key, and errors follow the contract above. Whether it is built in M1 is still the engineering review's and then the author's call. Check: a repeated POST with one key creates one update.
  - Measuring it: the second Triage person does the clean-clone run once before M1 goes live and the time is written in the README. Check: the README states the measured time and date.

### Phase 3: Engineering review

Run by /autoplan on 2026-11-11, last, on the plan as amended by the three earlier phases (implementation hash d6504232).

#### Step 0. Scope challenge

- **What already exists.** No code. `README.md:1` is the repo's only source line. Each sub-problem maps to a platform feature or a standard library, not to code here: sign-in to the identity provider or cluster login, TLS to the Route, scheduling to CronJobs, mail to Graph or the relay, message parsing to Python's `email` package, storage to Postgres.
- **Minimum change.** The goal is met by M1 alone. M2 to M4 are additions the author approved one by one. Under the pipeline's rule this review does not reduce scope.
- **Complexity check.** About 60 files and about 10 modules [estimated] for M1 as amended. That trips the gate (8 files, 2 services). Structure question, auto-decided: the original arrangement stays, one deployable with three scheduled entry points. No smaller arrangement keeps the approved feature list. Pending remedies not decided here: uc3 (JSON form), d14 (M1 in two steps).
- **Search check.** [Layer 1] Transactional outbox for mail (`mail_send` written before the send), row locks for check-then-write, conditional updates for state changes, server-rendered pages with partial updates. All are standard. The CronJob `timeZone` field is stable since Kubernetes 1.27 per the Kubernetes documentation, read through a search on 2026-11-11, so the plan's hourly fallback is needed only on an older cluster.
- **TODOS cross-reference.** Four items exist (sources page, Teams, handled-need notice, CSV export). None blocks this plan.
- **Distribution check.** One image, built by a CI system that is still Open Question 6. The BuildConfig stopgap is named. No other artifact.

#### Section 1. Architecture

```
                    browser (Lead, Manager, Triage)
                               |
                         Route (TLS)
                               |
        +----------------------+----------------------+
        |  web app x2: pages (and JSON form if built) |
        |  session in cookie or database              |
        +-----+--------------------+------------------+
              |                    |
       policy module        domain module: assignments
       (who may see,        (post update, create ask, wait,
        who may do)          end, done; row lock; "may ask now")
              |                    |
              +---------+----------+
                        |
                    Postgres  <---- migration Job (named per image, runs first)
                        ^
        +---------------+-----------------+------------------+
        | five-minute job                 | scheduler (7:00) | daily job (6:00)
        | 1 catch-up  2 send  3 pull      | creates rows     | 1 figures 2 purge db
        | (own timeout + job_run each)    | via the domain   | 3 purge mailbox
        +-------+-----------+-------------+ module           +---------+
                |           |                                          |
          relay or Graph   Graph (shared mailbox) <--------------------+
                                  ^
              identity provider (OIDC: discovery, keys, token)   ServiceNow: links only
```

**Two shapes.** The plan's shape (A) lets each page handler, the scheduler and the poller write assignment, update and ask rows themselves. The second shape (B), sketched here, puts those writes behind one module:

```
assignments.post_update(assignment_id, author, status?, text, source, on_behalf_of?) -> Update
assignments.create_ask(assignment_id, asked_by, origin, clock_start?) -> Ask | Refused(reason)
assignments.set_wait(assignment_id, until, reason, by) / clear_wait(...)
assignments.end(assignment_id, by) / may_ask_now(assignment_id, today) -> bool
policy.visible_firedrills(person) / policy.can(person, action, object) -> bool
```

B is picked. It hides the lock, the "answer every open ask" rule, the stale arithmetic and burst suppression behind six calls, where A spreads them over every caller. B is also what makes a later JSON form thin.

**Red flags screened against the chosen shape.**
- Split ownership: three writers of ask rows (button, scheduler, sender's cancel). Fixed by B.
- Two ways to do one task: the HTML form and the JSON form. Kept from drifting by having both call the domain module.
- Hand-synced list: the role list in the plan and the routes in code. Fixed by generating the authorization test from the route table.
- Temporal decomposition: the five-minute job was "everything that happens every five minutes". Now three phases with their own outcomes, which is the smallest fix that keeps one CronJob.
- No flag found for shallow module, information leakage, pass-through or importable internals at this depth. They are checks for the code review.

**One production failure per integration.** Identity provider unreachable: nobody signs in, 503 page (handled). Graph throttled: pull phase times out, send phase already ran (handled by the phase order). Relay down two hours: mail is retried until expiry (handled). Database down: app's own 503 (handled). Credential expired: warned 30 days ahead (handled). CronJob suspended: absence alert (handled).

Issues: 13 (E1 to E12 less E10, plus E16 and E42). All applied.

#### Section 2. Code quality

No code exists, so this reviews the structure the plan implies. No shared-code extraction is proposed: there are no two authored callers to compare.

- One pure date function driven by a table of cases owns stale, waiting, overdue, working days and non-working dates. Every caller uses it.
- Error handling gaps closed by this phase: bounces, transient versus permanent send failures, an unmovable message, a hung pull.
- Naming is settled by the DX glossary. Nothing further.
- Over-build check: the in-app settings screen for a one-maintainer tool was flagged by the CEO review and stays, because the mail switches must be reachable in an incident without a deploy.
- The plan's ASCII diagrams in the CEO record predate these amendments. The architecture diagram above replaces the CEO one for the build.

Issues: 4 (E5, E10, E17, E26). All applied.

#### Section 3. Test review

Framework: none exists (`CLAUDE.md` has no testing section and the repo has no test files). The stack decision before M1 names it. This review proposes no framework.

```
CODE PATHS (proposed)                                  USER FLOWS
[+] domain: assignments                                [+] Boss: forward to board
  ├── post_update                                        ├── [GAP] [→E2E] paste → confirm → publish → row
  │   ├── [GAP] answers every open ask in one txn        ├── [GAP]        two people publish one draft
  │   ├── [GAP] no status → status unchanged             └── [GAP]        identical paste
  │   └── [GAP] done → another status reopens          [+] Boss: ask
  ├── create_ask                                         ├── [GAP] [→E2E] Request update → "Sent it?" → Asked
  │   ├── [GAP] lock: update lands mid-scheduler         ├── [GAP]        double click
  │   ├── [GAP] one automatic ask per stale period       └── [GAP]        Ask all → Cancel cancels the batch
  │   └── [GAP] suppressed after switch-on / lower N   [+] Manager: answer
  ├── set_wait / clear_wait                              ├── [GAP] [→E2E] status link → form → Send → confirmation
  │   └── [GAP] stale clock restarts after the wait      ├── [GAP]        six edge states
  └── may_ask_now  [GAP] table of cases                  ├── [GAP]        double Send
[+] dates: stale, waiting, overdue                       └── [GAP]        session expired with text typed
  └── [GAP] table: Fri, Sat, holiday, 23:30 boundary   [+] Manager: reply (M2)
[+] extraction rules                                     ├── [GAP] [→E2E] reply → update → receipt
  ├── [GAP] ticket prefixes, longest first               ├── [GAP]        automatic reply, bounce
  └── [GAP] due date: year roll-over, bad anchor         └── [GAP]        below the marker, wrong sender
[+] policy: visible / can                              [+] Triage: operate
  └── [GAP] generated from the route table               ├── [GAP]        job overdue shows on banner
[+] sessions, forgery, headers                           └── [GAP]        restore rehearsal
  └── [GAP] route-table walk, two-replica sign-in      [+] Error states
[+] jobs                                                 ├── [GAP]        503 outage page
  ├── [GAP] each job run twice                           ├── [GAP]        401 on a partial update
  ├── [GAP] pull hung: send still runs                   └── [GAP]        no-access and no-role pages
  ├── [GAP] retry until expiry, bounce is final
  └── [GAP] unmovable message does not starve
[+] migration Job  [GAP] failure leaves old version up
[+] model pass (M3)  [GAP] [→EVAL] real-email set against the rules-only baseline

COVERAGE: 0/38 paths tested (0%)  |  Code paths: 0/19  |  User flows: 0/19
QUALITY: none exist  |  GAPS: 38 (4 E2E, 1 eval)
```

Legend: [GAP] no test exists | [→E2E] needs an end-to-end test | [→EVAL] needs a model evaluation

Every gap is required proof of behavior the plan already accepts, so none needs a separate decision. All 38 go into the build as tests written with the code. No regression rule applies: there is no existing behavior to protect. Evaluation scope (M3 only): the private real-email set, compared with the rules-only result, run by hand before each release.

Tests made obsolete by this plan: none.

Test plan artifact: `~/.gstack/projects/pcarpe4-situation-room/patrick-main-eng-review-test-plan-20261111-165000.md`.

Issues: 38 gaps, all accepted as tests to write. Findings E29, E30, E31, E32 applied.

#### Section 4. Performance

Scale: tens of firedrills, under 20 people, tens of mails a day. Unknown beyond that.

- The board is the one hot path. Budget: one page render uses a fixed number of queries (latest update, open asks, open needs and seen marks fetched once for the page, not per row). A test counts queries for a 30-row fixture and fails above the fixed number. No time budget is set, since no hardware is known.
- Row locks are held for one short transaction per assignment. No contention at this scale.
- The poller's per-run cap and the sender's per-run limit bound each job's runtime.
- Connections: two replicas and three jobs against one small Postgres. The pool size is a configuration key, sized in M1a.
- Indexes owed are those the CEO review listed, plus the partial unique indexes above.

Issues: 2 (board query count has no test, pool size unset). Both applied as checks.

#### Failure modes

| Path | Realistic failure | Test? | Handled? | User sees |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Ask beside an update | Ask commits after the answering update | named | row lock | nothing wrong |
| Switch on, lower N | Burst of asks | named | stored markers | no mail |
| Sign-in on two replicas | Handshake lost | named | shared session | signs in |
| Changing request without token | Forged post | route walk | refused | error page |
| Manager reads another's firedrill | Id guessed | generated | policy module | refused |
| CronJob never runs | Suspended, image pull fails | named | absence alert | banner, alert |
| Mailbox pull hangs | Graph throttled | named | phase timeout | mail still sent |
| Relay down two hours | Transient errors | named | retry to expiry | late mail |
| Bounce | Dead address | named | final for that ask | "Ask failed", reassign |
| Credential expires | Fixed date passes | named | 30-day warning | Health warning |
| Migration fails | Half-applied | named | transaction, old version stays | nothing |
| Restore | Replies lost, mail repeated | rehearsal | runbook | some asks repeated, stated |
| Email with script | Markup in a field | named | escape, CSP | text |
| Model pass (M3) | Invented quote | named | substring check | empty field |

Critical gaps: 0 after the amendments. Before them there was 1: a CronJob that never runs had no test, no handling and no visible sign except to someone opening the Health page.

<!-- autoplan-accepted:eng -->
- **Engineering amendments accepted by the /autoplan engineering pass on 2026-11-11.** They say how the rules above are built so that they hold under two replicas, three jobs and a restore. Where one refines an earlier amendment, it says so. Two are marked provisional: the author confirms or reverses them at the final gate.
  - One owner for assignment changes: a single domain module owns every change to an assignment (post an update, create an ask, set or clear a wait, end, mark done). Pages, jobs and the poller call it and never write those tables directly. Each of those operations locks the assignment row first and recomputes staleness under the lock, so an ask can never commit just after the update that answered the others. "May this assignment be asked now" is one function, read by the button, the scheduler and the digest. Check: a test on real Postgres pauses the scheduler between its read and its write, lets an update land, and ends with no open ask.
  - Guards on every transition: publish, attach, Handled, Close, Reopen, cancel an ask and Send are conditional updates that check the state they expect and the number of rows changed, and each form carries a one-time token under a unique constraint. Partial unique indexes enforce one unended assignment per firedrill and owner, one update per reply message id, one automatic ask per assignment and `clock_start`, and one digest per Monday. Removing the last unended owner is refused. Check: two simultaneous publishes of one draft yield one firedrill, and a repeated Send yields one update.
  - Burst suppression is stored: when the global switch, a firedrill's `auto_ask`, N or the non-working dates change, the app writes a cancelled automatic ask for every assignment that is stale at that moment. The unique key then suppresses that stale period, and the retry of unsent asks skips cancelled ones. Check: switch on with three stale assignments, run 7:00 twice, zero mail.
  - Fields added: `update_request.confirmed_sent_at` (the "Sent it?" answer), `update_request.batch_id` (one "Ask all" click), an automatic-reply time on the ask, and a `banner_dismissal` table keyed on the source row. `mail_send` points at what it is about through separate nullable keys with a check that exactly one is set, and an ask's latest send is derived, not stored on the ask. Check: the first migration holds these and the checks.
  - "No update yet" is shown when an assignment has no update, not when its status is `new`, so a first reply that names no status does not read as silence. Check: a status-less first reply shows its text with status "No update yet" replaced by "status not given".
  - Jobs in phases: the five-minute job runs catch-up, then send, then pull, each with its own time limit, error handling and `job_run` row, so a hung mailbox pull cannot stop mail going out. The daily job computes the Friday figures first, then purges the database, then the mailbox copies, each with its own outcome. A late freshness figure is a snapshot at the late run and is marked so. Check: with the mailbox unreachable, pending mail is still sent and the figures are still stored.
  - A job that never runs: an alert rule fires when a CronJob's last success is older than twice its interval. Without cluster alerting, any page load by a Lead or Triage evaluates the same test and raises the banner. This completes the "overdue" state. Check: suspend the five-minute CronJob and the banner appears within two intervals.
  - Sending, refined: transient failures (connect, sign-in to the relay, throttling) are retried with backoff until the mail's expiry, honoring Retry-After. This replaces "three times over an hour". Permanent failures fail at once. A bounce is permanent for that ask: it is not sent again and the row asks for a new owner or address. Sending health is judged on transport errors, not on one recipient's rejection. Each run sends at most a set number of mails. The Health page's retry only sets a row back to pending, and only a job sends. Check: a two-hour relay outage at 7:00 delivers that morning's asks afterwards, and a bounced ask is not re-sent the next day.
  - Sessions and sign-in: the session and the sign-in handshake values live in a signed, encrypted cookie or in the database, never in process memory, and the app reads the person's active flag and role on every request. The registration is single-tenant with issuer and tenant checked. The first sign-in binds the provider's immutable id to the person and later sign-ins match on it, through a maintained OIDC library. Under OIDC every forwarded-user header is ignored. Under oauth-proxy the app listens on localhost only, the Service exposes the proxy port only, and a NetworkPolicy closes the rest. The address to return to after sign-in must be a relative path. Check: a sign-in that starts on one replica and returns on the other succeeds, and a request with a forged user header under OIDC is anonymous.
  - Roles from the directory use app roles (the `roles` claim), not the groups claim, because a person in many groups receives no group list. A claim never demotes the last active Triage person. Under OIDC each person's role carries the date it was last confirmed at sign-in, and mailbox intake accepts a sender only when that date is within 30 days. Provisional, because a Lead who has not signed in for 30 days would have a forwarded email refused. Check: a person in more than 200 groups signs in with the right role, and a sender whose role was confirmed 31 days ago is listed as not accepted with that reason.
  - Request forgery: every request that changes something carries a token and passes an Origin check, cookies are SameSite=Lax, and a test walks the route table so a new route without the check fails the suite. Check: a POST without the token is refused on every route.
  - One visibility policy: a single module answers which firedrills a person may see and whether a person may do an action to an object, denying by default, and every handler uses it. The answers to Open Questions 2 and 15 are named configuration keys. The test of role, ownership and each variant is generated from the route table, so an unlisted route fails. Check: with the restricted setting, a manager's request for another manager's firedrill by id is refused on every route.
  - Text from email: it is escaped first and highlights are added by position afterwards. The app sends a Content-Security-Policy with no inline script. Ticket links are built only from tokens the prefix rule matched, and the ServiceNow address must be https. Line breaks are stripped from anything placed in a mail subject, and every part of a mailto address is percent-encoded. Check: a script tag in every email-derived field, including the quote-highlight path, renders as text.
  - Mailto size: a test holds the encoded length of the ask under a set budget (1,800 characters until the M0 proof gives a real limit), and above it the ask falls back to one link. "Ask all stale owners" is held to the same budget. Check: a 200-character title still produces a link under the budget.
  - Mailbox intake, refined: messages are keyed on the mail system's immutable id, with the Message-ID header as a second signal, since a sender controls that header. Ids already handled are filtered out before the per-run cap is applied, a failed move is retried on its own and alerts after repeated failure, and the row and its effects commit in one transaction before the move. Only text parts are stored. Attachment names are recorded and attachments are not kept. One level of "forwarded as attachment" is unwrapped. Protected or encrypted mail is not accepted, with its own reason. A size cap applies. Check: one unmovable message does not stop newer mail from being handled.
  - Who sent a reply: the app accepts a reply only when the mail system marks it internal and authenticated, matches on the From address, and does not accept a reply sent on behalf of someone else. The reference in an ask is derived from the assignment with a keyed hash, is not guessable, and does not depend on the ask's row. At most ten email-reply updates are posted per assignment per day, and the rest are filed as not accepted. Check: a reply with a made-up reference posts nothing, and a reply to an ask whose row was lost in a restore still posts.
  - Reply stripping has a fixed fallback: the ask carries a "reply above this line" marker and the text is cut there first. If what remains still holds a From and Sent header block or is longer than a set length, nothing is posted and the manager gets the notice with the update link. Check: a reply typed below the marker posts nothing and draws the notice.
  - Restore, refined: the runbook's test is that the three replies are posted, no receipt is sent again during the replay, and any ask sent after the backup may be sent once more, which is accepted in writing. This replaces the earlier "no duplicate ask". Check: the rehearsal records how many asks were repeated.
  - Deploying: the migration Job is named for the image it runs, the pipeline applies it, waits for it to finish, and only then applies the Deployment. Readiness means the process is up, and the app serves its own 503 page when the database is down. One required configuration key holds the app's public address, checked at start, and every link in mail is built from it. The read-only part of the after-deploy check (health, database, a board for a seeded role) is a script the pipeline runs. Check: a deploy with a failing migration leaves the old version serving, and a wrong public address stops the app at start.
  - Requests added to M0: outbound access from the cluster to the identity provider's discovery, key and token addresses for M1, and to the token address as well as Graph for M2. An eighth tenant proof: the app's credential tries to read a different mailbox and is refused. The app registration uses a certificate or workload identity where offered. Its expiry date is configuration, the Health page warns 30 days ahead, and the alert rules include it. Check: the M0 request list names the provider addresses, and the eighth proof has a recorded result.
  - Due dates, closing earlier concerns R3-7 and R3-8: a date with no year is filled only when it falls in the calendar year of its anchor, and a date that would roll into the next year is listed as a candidate. A Sent line that is missing, unreadable or numeric and ambiguous is no anchor. Check: "the January 15 deadline was missed" in a November message fills nothing.
  - Retention, closing gaps: the audit log stores field names and ids for email-derived fields, not their values. The backup window is stated beside the retention periods, because backups keep deleted content for that long. Raw replies are readable by Lead and Triage only. Supporting quotes show on the confirm screen only. Opening an original email is written to the audit log. Check: after the purge, no table outside backups holds the email's text.
  - Small rules: a wait date must be in the future. A wait ends when its assignment is done. Publishing a draft the purge already deleted is refused with a message. A Lead's own view of an update on their own assignment does not mark it seen. The last board view time is written after the page is rendered. Refused sign-ins are stored once per value per day. Text typed before a session expired is kept in the browser and restored after sign-in, and no request is replayed. The development sign-in also refuses to start unless the app listens on localhost, and a check in CI fails if any manifest sets its flag. Check: one test per rule.
  - Tests run on Postgres with the app's clock: CI uses the compose file's Postgres, never SQLite, and every business timestamp comes from the app's settable clock, not from the database. Synthetic or redacted email fixtures live in the repo for CI. The private set of real emails is run by hand before each release and the result is recorded. Check: CI fails if a table default uses the database clock for a business timestamp.
  - Milestone 1 in two steps: M1a is a deployed walking skeleton (sign-in, roster, a firedrill created by hand, the board, the update form and the mailto ask), which shows the sign-in value, whether the route can be reached and whether the pipeline works in the first week. M1b is the rest of M1. Nothing is cut. Provisional, because it changes how the author laid out the milestones. Check: M1a is deployed before M1b work on intake starts.
  - The JSON form: the pages call the domain module above, so a JSON form can be added thinly whenever it gets a caller. Whether it is built in M1 is not decided here. Three reviewers in this run advised adding it with its first caller, and the author asked for it, so the choice is the author's at the final gate. Until then M1 includes it. Check: no page handler holds a rule that the domain module does not.

~~~~

### Phase 3 rerun: Engineering review after the final gate (cycle 1)

**Why it ran.** The author replied `rec` at the final gate on 2026-11-11. That keeps the app-first plan and the Milestone 2 order, confirms every provisional amendment, and takes the one recommended change: the JSON form leaves Milestone 1 and is added with its first caller. The change was applied through five recorded baseline edits (the Shape paragraph twice, the API paragraph, the architecture diagram label, the estimate note) and a revised line in the engineering amendments. The engineering review then ran again on the changed plan with a fresh independent reviewer.

**Step 0. Scope challenge.** Nothing in the repo solves any sub-problem: there is still no code. The change removes work (no `/api/` routes, OpenAPI document, idempotency keys or JSON authorization tests in M1) and adds none. **Section 1. Architecture.** 13 issues (A1 to A6, S2, S4, S5, H1, H2, H3, E5). The shape from the first pass stands: one domain module owns assignment changes, and pages, jobs and the poller call it. Removing the JSON form removes the "two ways to do one task" flag recorded in the first pass. No new module or boundary is introduced, so design-twice does not run again.

```
  Browser (Lead, Triage, Manager)
        |  HTML pages and partial updates only in M1
        v
  Route (TLS) --> sign-in (mode: oidc | proxy | dev)
        |
  web app x2: page handlers --> visibility policy --> domain module --> Postgres
        ^                                                ^        ^
        |  (JSON form: later, same module)               |        |
  CronJobs: five-minute (catch-up, send, pull) ----------+        |
            daily (figures, purge with ceiling) ------------------+
            7:00 scheduler (one success per date, ask cap)
```

One production failure per changed path: a wrong clock setting in production would make the purge delete early. Handled now by refusing the override outside `dev` and by the purge ceiling.

**Section 2. Code quality.** 4 issues (A1 as a documentation defect, H5, A6, E8). No shared-code extraction is proposed: there is no code and the single domain module is already the shared owner.
